scan reads the resting height and the support off the skyline and
asks the validator for the plausible poses only; its own window of
1 s past the resort's deadline, the call capped at the budget plus
2 s).

**The grid rescue measured** (`grid2-*` against the v43 records; the
v43 form plus `grid_rescue=0.05, grid_rescue_seconds=1.0`):

| suite | crossings | placed | grid placements | policy max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| hard2 C | 32 -> 36 (sample 28 -> 31, stretch 4 -> 5) | +1.83 (17 up, 6 down) | 208 in 37 episodes | 16.0 s (s0091, the stack option) |
| hard2 B | 37 -> 38 (sample 30 -> 29, stretch 7 -> 9) | +0.44 | 3 in 2 | 18.0 s (s0091) |
| hard C | 21 -> 19 | -0.52 (8 up, 13 down) | 128 in 19 | 6.5 s |
| hard B | 21 -> 19 | +1.04 (10 up, 11 down) | 5 in 3 | 7.4 s |
| core C | 48 -> 48 | +0.02 | | 6.3 s |
| core B | 48 -> 48 | -0.27 | | 7.3 s |

On B the stage barely fires (the resort spends the window and the
cap stops the scan), so B's swings are the pool's known noise
(b-hard-s0041 30 -> 71, b-hard-s0025 65 -> 49).  On C the losses are
not the grid's either: of the four crossings that moved on the hard
suites, three episodes diverged at a pocket-guard decision at steps
2-10, long before any grid placement (the guard's time budget), and
the fourth where the resort found nothing in the new run and the grid
placed instead.  The time-faithful runs cannot attribute a change of
one or two crossings; `v43det-hard2`, `grid2det-hard2` and
`sealdet-hard2` (`deterministic_budgets`) are the attribution runs.
The stack option's 16-18 s calls on s0091 are v43's own
(`stack_deadline`, measured as `sdl-*`).

**The sealing design measured** (`seal-*`: the grid rescue plus
`rescue_deep_first` and `rescue_seal_area=0.2`, against the v43
records).  The rescue stages' lowest-first order fills the door side
(74-87 % of the v43 rescue placements stand in the front 0.45 m, 58-69
% on end, and 30-41 of 48 episodes a suite end within four steps of
one), and the simulator's sweep enters at the low-y side along the
target's x, so a tall pose there walls off every lower surface behind
it; deep-first tries the poses by depth band (0.25 m) before height,
and the sealing pass holds the poses that wall off 0.2 m^2 or more of
usable surface back to a second pass run only when nothing else stood.

| suite | crossings | placed | margin 0/+1 | priced | policy max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core C | 48 -> 48 | +1.27 (24 up, 10 down) | 5 -> 6 | fill +0.11 soft +0.05 prio +0.05 cog -0.43 -> -0.22 | 6.1 s |
| core B | 48 -> 48 | +0.21 (15 up, 15 down) | 1 -> 2 | -0.10 | 7.4 s |
| hard C | 21 -> 25 | +0.79 (25 up, 10 down) | 1 -> 5 | | 6.3 s |
| hard B | 21 -> 20 | +0.35 | 5 -> 3 | | 7.4 s |
| hard2 C | 32 -> 39 (sample 28 -> 35, stretch 4 -> 4) | +1.73 / +3.34 (sample / stretch) | 4 -> 6 | | 16.0 s (s0091, the stack option) |
| hard2 B | 37 -> 37 | +0.00 / -0.16 | 4 -> 5 | | 17.8 s (s0091) |

The crossings gained on hard C are the near misses the probe
diagnosed (s0048, s0042, s0037, s0038, s0033, s0013, plus s0018 60 ->
78); the crossings lost are large swings on two- and three-container
scenes (c-hard-s0025 62 -> 47, s0007 64 -> 47, c-hard2-s0041 77 ->
41, s0022 133 -> 106) that diverge at a pocket-guard or resort
decision at steps 6-26, and three of the six end on `transport` (the
simulator refusing a resort pose the shadow had passed: 3 such ends
under v43 on hard2 C, 4 here).  B is flat: the stages barely fire with
a pool, and the settle endings rise 5 -> 10 on hard2 B.  The
continuous pricing on core C reads -0.22 (more covers, a higher
centre of mass); the count reads +1.27 a scene with every crossing
kept, which on the platform has been worth about a point a placed
item (v43).  The attribution runs under `deterministic_budgets`
(`v43det-`, `grid2det-`, `sealdet-hard2`: the same scene gives the
same episode) separate the design from the time noise, on hard2 C:

| form | crossings (sample / stretch) | placed sample | placed stretch |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| v43 | 35 (30 / 5) | 41.58 | 44.69 |
| grid rescue alone | 38 (33 / 5) | -0.02 (12 up, 5 down) | +0.84 (9 up, 2 down) |
| grid + deep-first + sealing pass | 39 (35 / 4) | +1.21 (23 up, 6 down) | +1.94 (17 up, 3 down) |

The grid rescue alone buys three crossings in the sample band at no
count; the order and the sealing pass add two more and the count
(+1.23 a scene over the grid alone, 18 up and 10 down), and give one
back in the stretch band.  Shipped as v44 on Tasks B and C with
`stack_deadline` on every task.

**v44 on the platform: 53.13** (v43 55.24; fill -0.17, cog -3.63,
stability -4.09, placement -2.05, soft -0.80, the placed fraction
0.6370 -> 0.6348).  The stability is v42's to the second decimal
(75.19), so the one platform episode v43 lifted over the threshold
fell back under it while the count elsewhere held (v42's fraction was
0.614).  The suites could not see it: sample C +1.27 with 48 of 48,
hard C +4 crossings, hard2 C +7.  What moved that episode is the
reordering of the rescue's poses from the first rescue placement on
(the deep-first order and the sealing pass change the course of every
episode the rescue touches); the grid rescue acts only where v43
declines, so an episode's v43 count is its floor under it, apart from
the time budgets.  v45 keeps the grid rescue and the stack deadline
and drops the other two.  The platform's edge episode is the one
that decides; the hard suites, built for crossings, measure a
different population, and the sample suite's 48 of 48 hides the
edge.

**Task A on the hard suite: what stops the feasible scenes.**  The A
agent orders the manifest, so the threshold's bound is the volume of
the smallest threshold items over the containers' (need_small,
`scripts/hard_why.py` on `a-*` records): under 30 % the v39 A form
crosses 25 of 36, at 30-40 % 1 of 8, above 40 % 0 of 4 (26 of 48;
re-measured as `abase-hard-a`: 24 of 48, the A episodes move too).
The misses are not the plan's size: through the agent's own optimize
the planner reaches the threshold on a-hard-s0029 (19 planned of 31,
threshold 16; the episode placed 10), 92 of 144 on s0033 (threshold
73, placed 71), 47 on s0047 (49, placed 46).  The soft headroom
slack is inert (0.05 / 0.09 / 0.13: the same plans to the item).
The loss is the replay: on the hard A records only 0.33 of the
placements replay the plan and the ladder improvises from step 4
(median) on, crossed and missed scenes alike; the shipped A form
replays with `plan_replay_nudge=0.0` (the config default is 0.02),
so a neighbour that settled a few millimetres off fails the next
planned pose and the plan is gone.  Measured on hard A against
`abase-hard-a` (48 scenes, the official 150 s budget):

| form | crossings | placed | replayed share | first improvisation (median) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| abase (v39 A form) | 24 | 44.75 | 0.33 | 4 |
| `plan_replay_nudge=0.02` | 25 | +3.46 (21 up, 8 down) | 0.61 | 17.5 |
| `soft_headroom_slack=0.09` | 25 | +0.38 | | |
| both | 25 | +2.56 | | |

The nudge doubles the replay and adds 3.5 items a scene; the
crossings move by one because most misses sit 3-20 under the
threshold (ten scenes within 6 after the nudge: s0031 47/48, s0038
36/37, s0019 62/64, s0016 39/41, s0047 46/49, s0013 57/61, s0039,
s0008, s0040, s0029).  Where a replayed plan loses to the ladder's
improvisation (a-hard-s0046 63 -> 51, the plan replayed from step 9
where the base improvised) the plan itself is the weaker play.
Policy max 6.54 s.  The variants on the nudge (hard A, against
`anudge-hard-a`): `plan_prefer_planner` 26 crossings, -0.31 a scene;
`plan_replay_drop=0.03` (the settled-height replay) 27 crossings,
-0.17, policy max 5.08 s; a 4 cm nudge 24 crossings, -1.79 (6 up, 14
down) -- 2 cm is the size.  On the sample A suite (`anudge-core-a`
against `abase-core-a`): placed +0.96 (16 up, 8 down), soft +0.54
(13 up, 1 down), priority +0.06, 48 of 48 crossings (the margin-0/+1
episodes 8 -> 7), priced +0.14 (fill +0.09, soft +0.14, priority
+0.06, cog -0.14), policy max 4.37 s; the shake topples rise 7 -> 12
over the 48 scenes (two more scenes topple: a-c2p-s0008 three items,
a-c2p-s0009 two), the one cost to watch on the platform's stability
component.  With the drop too (`adrop-core-a`): placed +0.98 (17 up, 8 down),
soft +0.54, 48 of 48, priced +0.18 (cog -0.11), topples 7 -> 12 as
with the nudge alone, policy max 4.39 s.  The A form for v46:
`plan_replay_nudge=0.02, plan_replay_drop=0.03`.

**What the plans lack on the hard A near misses** (`plan_view`, the
v46 arm through the agent's optimize, 60 s): the plans replay in full
there (s0013 52 of 52, s0039 22 of 22, s0016 37 of 37, s0008 22 of
22) and are themselves 4-10 under the threshold.  Two structures.
The soft headroom reserve is sized by the soft heights whatever the
soft count, so the hard rows stop 0.45 m under the ceiling on a
manifest of 5 soft boxes (s0039: 6 872 `cap` refusals, 22 of 28) and,
where the soft cargo is many, the soft pass then fails to use the
reserved layer (uneven tops, the sweep, the 0.60 m soft boxes: s0013
15 781 cap refusals, 33 soft unplanned, 52 of 61; s0047 41 soft
unplanned, 45 of 49; s0003 10 388 cap refusals, 13 of 26).  And in a
priority container the priority cargo takes the floor first and
nothing may stand on it (s0040: 3 851 `covers-other-attribute`, 11 of
21, half the floor at 0.2-0.5 m).  Three options, measured against
the v46 arm (`adrop-*`):

| form | hard A crossings | hard A placed | sample A placed | sample A soft / priority | priced (A's third) | topples |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `soft_headroom_volume_cap=1.2` | 27 -> 28 | +0.33 | +0.31 (5 up, 1 down) | +0.02 / +0.06 | -0.14 (cog -0.20) | 12 -> 9 |
| `plan_variants_semicolon` + `plan_priority_on_top` | 28 | +1.15 (11 up, 6 down) | +0.75 (19 up, 8 down) | -0.29 / -0.60 | -0.85 (priority -0.32, cog -0.59) | 13 |
| both | 27 | +0.50 | +1.00 (21 up, 9 down) | -0.42 / -0.58 | -1.02 | 13 |

The variants buy count with the priority cargo (its share 0.81 ->
0.74) and a higher load: on Task A every sample episode crosses, so
the platform's components scale with the quality, not the count, and
the continuous pricing is the judge there; neither ships.  The cap is
mild either way (48 of 48, the margin-0/+1 episodes 7 -> 3).
`plan_reserve_variants=on;off` (`ares-*`, against `adrop-*`): hard A
crossings 27 -> 26 (a-hard-s0038 44 -> 36), placed +0.90 (13 up, 3
down); sample A placed +0.75 (13 up, 1 down) with soft -0.79 (the
share 0.52 -> 0.46), cog -0.55, priced -0.63, topples 12 -> 17.  The
plan without the reserve wins the count with the soft cargo, which is
what the reserve was for (v19 on the platform: -2.61).  None of the
three ships; v46's replay is the A step, and the next A lever is the
quality (the load's height, the soft share), not the count: on A the
sample suite crosses 48 of 48 under every form here.

The mass order asked for (heavy cargo low) has no handle in the row
planner: a row is a stack of one depth class, so its layers are one
SKU and one mass, and the slot choice under count_first takes the
smallest footprint that fits whatever the planning order; the load's
height is decided by the row depths the layout search picks and the
layers they take, not by the order the items are offered in.
With the alternatives gated to the base plan's shortfall
(`plan_alt_gate=base-under`, all four options on; `agate-*`): hard A
27 -> 25, sample A placed -1.23 with soft -1.27 -- the gate keeps the
first *variant* planned, which with the ";" split is the priority-first
order, not the shipped mixed order, so the base itself moved.  The A
planner thread closes here: every count lever on hard A costs the
sample suite's soft or priority share, and on A the quality is the
score.

**v46 on the platform: 55.34**, the best so far (v43 55.24, v45
55.21): cog +0.62 against v45, fill -0.04, stability +0.03,
placement and soft unchanged, the placed fraction 0.6370 -> 0.6363.
The A replay lowered the load (the planned poses the ladder's
improvisation had replaced) and left the count and the shares where
they were; on A the quality is what moves the score.

**The feasible set on the hard suites** (the target: every scene whose
demand is under 0.40 of the containers' volume crosses; the demand is
the first-threshold volume on B and C, the smallest-threshold volume
on A, the agent's fill reaches 0.33 of the containers on average and
0.43-0.50 at most).  Crossings by demand band under the v46/v47 forms:

| suite | under 0.30 | 0.30-0.35 | 0.35-0.40 | 0.40 and over | feasible misses |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| hard A (v46) | 26/36 | 0/3 | 1/5 | 0/4 | 17 |
| hard C (v47) | 13/19 | 5/7 | 1/9 | 0/13 | 16 |
| hard B (v47) | 12/19 | 4/7 | 3/9 | 0/13 | 16 |
| hard2 C (v47) | 19/24 | 14/27 | 1/21 | 2/24 | 38 |
| hard2 B (v47) | 20/24 | 14/27 | 4/21 | 2/24 | 34 |

The misses within one to four of the threshold: hard2 C s0039,
s0028, s0026, s0010, s0007 (-1), s0057 (-2), s0065, s0048, s0027
(-3), s0064, s0013, s0006, s0004 (-4); hard2 B s0059, s0047, s0014,
s0007 (-1), s0077, s0051, s0039, s0026, s0018, s0009 (-2); hard C
s0048, s0044, s0042 (-1), s0038 (-2); hard B s0033 (-1), s0031, s0025
(-2); hard A s0019, s0016 (-2), s0047, s0039, s0013 (-4).  The
0.35-0.40 band (1/21 and 4/21 on hard2) asks a fill the agent reaches
only on its best scenes.

**The checkmate guard** (`checkmate_guard`: the last choice before a
checkmate -- when a stage's pick would leave no usable patch for any
next item (the pool on B, the two commonest hard classes seen on C)
and another candidate in the first six would, that one goes first;
read with the usable-surface instrument at 4 cm; on the ladder's
survivors, the physics resort's and the grid rescue's candidates).
Local runs on the near misses: c-hard-s0048 22 checks, 1 trigger, 1
override, 20 placed as before; c-hard-s0042 55 checks, 2 triggers, no
override (no alternative left a place either), 52 as before;
c-hard2-s0039 and s0028 no trigger; b-hard2-s0059 1 trigger, s0014 6
triggers, no override, counts as before.  The guard fires only when
every alternative is dead too: the move that seals the board is made
while usable surface still remains, so a one-move reading cannot see
it, and the multi-move reading is what the session's learning
experiments showed to be unpredictable from the state.  Off; not
measured on CI.

**A learned gate for the sealing design** (`scripts/gate_learn.py`: at
every third step of the baseline C episodes on hard2 and the sample
suite, the usable surface ratio, the free floor share, the placed
count over 41 and the container count; the label is the episode
ending under the count threshold; 1 971 states from 144 episodes, 60
of them under; logistic, leave-scenes-out).  AUC 0.63.  The weights
are the free floor 0.45, the placed count 0.43, the containers 0.50
and the usable surface 0.03: the model reads the scene's size, not
the position's risk.  At p > 0.5 it opens on 6 % of the sample
suite's steps and on 38 % of the hard2 steps of episodes that cross
against 49 % of those that end under, from a tenth of the episode on;
the hand gate (usable surface under 0.2) opens on 30 % of the sample
steps and 20 % of hard2's, late.  Within hard2 the fate is not
separable from the state mid-episode (0.38 against 0.49), as every
multi-step reading in this session has found; the low-phase gate
stays the gate.

**v45 on the platform: 55.21** (v43 55.24): the placed fraction and
the fill are v43's to the fifth decimal, the other components within
0.1 (the settle's noise).  The grid rescue placed nothing there: the
platform's declines have no legal pose left (sealed or full), unlike
the hard suites' near misses, where the generators had missed legal
poses.  The stack deadline cost nothing.

**Task B's pool against Task C's stream.**  On the hard suites, the
same seeds under B and C: with a pool of 10 or 20 B places 3.5 and 4.4
items a scene more than C, with a pool of 3 or 5 1.5 and 0.5 fewer;
the suite's B average hides the pool sizes.  B's episodes end with 30
to 170 items still in the stream, every item of the pool without a
pose (b-hard-s0036: 28 of 175 placed, 127 in the stream, none of the
20 visible placeable), and the agent does use the pool's depth (65 %
of its choices at the fifth visible item or deeper on the sample's
pool of ten): the room ends, not the choice.

**The pool order by fit** (`pool_order_mode` = fit: the visible item
whose flattest footprint wastes the least of a free floor rectangle
that exists now goes first, small boxes into the strips and big boxes
while the floor has their rectangle; items with no floor rectangle in
the size order after; `poolfit-hard-b`, `poolfit-core-b` against the
v39 arms): negative on both suites.  Sample B placed -0.96 a scene (17
up, 23 down; b-c2p-s0011 -18), soft -0.52, priority -0.29, 48 -> 46
crossings, -0.81 priced; hard B 19 -> 14 crossings (six lost, one
gained), placed -1.52, and the loss sits on the pools of 10 and 20
(5 -> 2 and 7 -> 5 crossings) while the pool of 3 gained one.  The
order sends the big boxes to the open floor first (their waste there
is the least), and the ladder's rows and terraces, built from the
small cargo it would have taken first, never form; the soft cargo
waits longer still.  Not adopted; the switch stays at `size`.  What
the B end states say stands: the pool's choice is not what binds, the
room is, and a pool strategy has to make room (rows of one depth from
the pool's items) rather than pick the item for the room that is
left.

**The count pass before every decline** (`count_mode_always`, the
cover-allowed resort whatever the 41-based estimate says;
`countalways-hard` against `v38-hard`, `countalways-core` against
`softres-core`): on the hard C suite crossings 16 -> 17 (s0033 and
s0038 gained, s0023 lost), placed -0.12 a scene -- the pass running
past the estimate is not what the hard scenes lack; the floor is.
On the scenes both arms cross it costs what it always costs,
contact-covered boxes 22 -> 54 and CoM +0.021 (-0.46 points) for fill
+2.3.  On the sample C suite placed +2.31 a scene (36 up, 4 down),
fill +2.7, soft +0.75, priority +0.35, against priority covered 9 ->
34, soft covered 4 -> 29, CoM +0.015 and one crossing lost (c-c2-s0011
-9): -0.08 net under the continuous pricing.  Not adopted; the
estimate's error at 55-70 items a container is real but the pass is
not the remedy.

### What the top of the leaderboard is made of, and a layer packer that was not it

The leaderboard's top sits in the seventies.  The official evaluator
snapshot scores only the fill (the item volume inside over the
container's volume) and the count; the other four components are
described in the README, whose example feedback is the one hard data
point: a run that placed every item (`num_placed_items` 1.0) with
fill 15.3 scored cog 66.8, stability 72.1, placement 80 and soft 100.
At the weights, 70+ needs either nearly every item placed (fill at
the manifest's 50-56 %, placement and soft near 100) or the special
cargo complete on a low, stable load.  The items of a bench A manifest
sum to 50-56 % of the container and a level-layer shelf packing with
no rules fits 36-41 of 41 (`scripts/layers_probe.py`'s first form),
so the first reading was tried: `rule_alpha/layers.py`, an offline
packer building the load as rows from the back wall, every row
stacked to the ceiling, poses through the planner's pose check.

It does not beat the ladder: 20-25 of 41 against the dry-run's 25-30,
and the reasons are the simulator's carry-in rules, which the raw
geometry ignored.  The simulator carries an item in at its own height
(no lift) when its top lands within 0.098 m under the mid-height plane
(0.805 m) or the ceiling, or its bottom within 0.05 m above the
mid-height shelf level, and the item then scrapes the box it lands on
(130 of 135 third-level poses failed the sweep); the small shelf's
plate at mid-height along the left wall stops any column under its
edge at two boxes; a standing box in a column keeps every wider box
off it.  A column of 0.25 m boxes holds two flat ones, then needs a
standing one or a 0.27 one to clear the band, and reaches four or
five boxes where the geometry promised six.  The ladder's count is
already what those rules allow of a greedy pack; a packer that beats
it needs a search over column compositions against the bands, which
is a project, not a change.  The module stays as an experiment
(`offline_planner=layers`, off).

The second reading fits the data better.  The cog score reads as
100 x (1 - the mass-weighted centre of mass over the container's
height) on the counted episodes: the suites' CoM ratio averaged over
the three tasks gives 63.6 for v32's form against the official 63.31,
and 63.2 for v36's against 63.23.  So a point of cog is 0.01 of CoM
ratio, 0.22 on the total, and a two-layer load (ratio 0.2) is worth
about +3; count beyond the threshold is worth its fill alone (0.3 a
box); and the special cargo's shares, at 14 a unit each, are the
lever: soft 0.45 -> 0.9 and priority 0.75 -> 0.95 across the tasks
is +8 to +10.  The plan search's soft-at-25 plans (23 items, 14 of 15
soft, 5 of 5 priority, the load as low or lower) are that lever on
Task A, held back only by the count margin the platform showed in
v34; Task B's pool (6.9 soft boxes left a scene) and Task C's
priority cargo are the same lever where the stream allows.

### What the platform's four components measure, read off seventeen builds

The public evaluator reports only the fill and the count; the other
four are described in the simulator README ("評価指標") and read here
against every build with an official result (v18 to v41) and the
suite records of the same form (`scripts/platform_predict.py`; the
build-to-record mapping in the agent's table below is approximate
where a build's change touched one task only).  Hypothesis H1: the
soft score is the share of the stream's soft items placed and not
under cargo of another attribute; the placement score the share of
the stream's priority items placed, not under cargo of another
attribute and in the priority container when one exists; the cog
score 100 x (1 - the mass-weighted centre of mass over the height);
all three over the counted episodes (zero under the count threshold),
and the denominators the whole stream, placed or not.

| build | official soft / H1 | official placement / H1 | official cog / H1 | official stability | stability / cog |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| v18 | 19.85 / 21.1 | 35.6 / 35.9 | 54.83 / 36.4 | 62.69 | 1.143 |
| v24 | 37.9 / 37.8 | 24.05 / 41.1 | 40.59 / 48.0 | 46.43 | 1.144 |
| v26 | 25.35 / 22.8 | 38.45 / 37.9 | 53.37 / 37.7 | 62.55 | 1.172 |
| v29 | 28.3 / 30.5 | 39.75 / 45.3 | 53.22 / 44.0 | 62.69 | 1.178 |
| v31 | 36.35 / 39.2 | 46.85 / 57.0 | 63.55 / 57.2 | 75.22 | 1.184 |
| v32 | 36.6 / 40.7 | 48.5 / 63.0 | 63.31 / 60.5 | 75.16 | 1.187 |
| v36 | 36.2 / 44.1 | 47.8 / 67.4 | 63.23 / 63.2 | 75.19 | 1.189 |
| v39 | 36.75 / 46.6 | 48.6 / 68.1 | 63.20 / 62.9 | 75.15 | 1.189 |
| v40 | 36.7 / 49.2 | 47.6 / 68.5 | 63.53 / 62.9 | 75.19 | 1.184 |
| v41 | 37.4 / 46.0 | 45.2 / 67.0 | 59.51 / 62.2 | 70.87 | 1.191 |

What holds:

1. *cog is exactly H1 once every episode is counted.*  From v36 on
   the suites' 100 x (1 - CoM ratio) over counted episodes is within
   0.3 of the official figure (63.2 / 63.23, 62.9 / 63.20); before
   v31 the suites counted far fewer episodes than the platform did
   (v18: 9 of 48 on C) and under-read it by 10-18.  A point of cog is
   0.01 of CoM ratio, 0.22 on the total.
2. *stability is a constant per counted episode.*  Official stability
   over official cog is 1.14-1.19 on every build from v18 to v41,
   whatever the load: stability is (counted share) x 75, as cog is
   (counted share) x 63.  The README's example run (every item placed,
   fill 15.3) read 72.1.  None of our shake readings tracks it (the
   v39 suites: 52.9 from the maximum displacement, 89.7 from the
   mean, 99.2 from the topples) and none needs to: it has not moved
   with any load change we made, only with the count threshold.
3. *soft and placement are H1 in structure and 10 and 20 points
   under it in level.*  The two builds that moved the official soft
   score moved it as H1 says: soft first (v24) +17 official against
   +16 H1, the count mode (v31) +8 against +9; and the early builds
   sit on H1 (v18 19.85 / 21.1, v26 25.35 / 22.8, v29 28.3 / 30.5).
   From v32 on the official soft stands at 36-37 and placement at
   45-49 while the suites' H1 rose from 40 / 63 to 49 / 68: every
   suite gain since v33 (the shade veto's C count, the resort's soft
   on soft on B, the two-deep gallery on A) came with a count the
   platform did not reproduce, and the platform's typed items are
   placed properly at a lower rate than the sample's at the same
   count fraction (0.61): its streams or its containers differ from
   the sample's -- more typed cargo late in the stream, a priority
   container with misrouting, or both; the sample cannot say which.
4. *So all four components scale with the count.*  cog and
   stability are the counted share times a constant; soft and
   placement are shares of the whole stream, so a decline at 60 % of
   it caps them near 60 whatever the load does with what it sees.
   The leaderboard's 71 is then not a special-cargo score beside a
   count like ours: at soft and placement near 90, nine of ten typed
   items of the stream stand placed and uncovered, which is nine of
   ten items of the stream, and a fill near the manifests' 50-56 %.
   The gap to the top is the count -- the 44 % of the container the
   end states leave empty ("The end states as data") -- and the
   special-cargo lever this document named earlier is that count
   seen through two of its components.

The ship rule follows from 2 and 4 with the weights: an episode
under the threshold loses 0.219 x 63 + 0.210 x 75 + its soft and
placement, about 2.4 points of the total per sixteen episodes, which
is what v41 paid.

### The spare optimize time: a search over Task A's plans

Every Task A episode ends on a decline with about 40 % of the items
unplaced, and the count is the plan's count: the dry-run defers the
items it declines to the tail, and the online ladder declines them
again on the real board.  The optimize budget is 150 s (the platform
allows 180) and a single-container manifest used 25-35 s of it; a
planner plan costs 3-4 s, a dry-run 12-20 s on 41 items, 60-100 s on
82.  What the spare time can buy
(`reports/bench/plan-search-explore.txt`, six c1/c1s scenes, the v34
arm): the dry-run over different orders gives very different plans,
and priced at the official weights (fill 28.7 a unit of volume share,
soft 14.1 and priority 14.3 a unit of share, counted only when the
plan holds the threshold plus one) the order the agent plays is rarely
the best one.

| scene | plan played (v34) | best variant | value |
|---|---|---|---:|
| a-c1-s0001 | planner 26 items, 5 of 15 soft (28.2) | soft after a quarter of the hard boxes: 23 items, 14 soft, 5 of 5 priority | 36.0 |
| a-c1-s0005 | planner 31, 7 soft (27.8) | count-always: 31 items, 5 soft (30.9); soft at half: 22, 8 soft (30.7) | 30.9 |
| a-c1s-s0002 | planner 27, 7 soft (26.9) | the planner's | 26.9 |
| a-c1s-s0010 | dry-run 22, 2 soft (18.4) | small hard first: 23 items, 3 soft, 4 of 5 priority | 23.9 |
| a-c1-s0011 | planner 30, 6 soft (27.8) | soft after a quarter: 23 items, 13 soft | 35.7 |
| a-c1s-s0006 | planner 22, 9 soft (24.8) | soft interleaved: 22 items, 4 soft, 3 of 3 priority | 27.7 |

The constructive order plays the soft cargo last, after the hard
boxes have taken the room, so the dry-run's plan carries 2-4 of 15
soft boxes; moved in after the first quarter of the hard boxes the
same ladder places 13-14 of 15, for seven fewer hard boxes, and a soft
box is worth about three hard boxes' fill at the platform's prices.
Soft first outright (15 of 15 soft, 17 items) loses the gate, and
random orders of the hard boxes are all worse than the constructive
one (26-28 items against 30).  Averaged over the six scenes the best
candidate is worth 4.5 value points an episode more than the plan
played, about +1.5 on the total if the replays hold.

`plan_search` (rule_alpha/offline.py `order_variant`): after the
planner and the base dry-run, the variants in `plan_search_orders` run
while the budget lasts (the expected best first; a run that may not
finish is not started), every plan is priced by `plan_value_weights`
with the gate at the threshold plus `plan_value_margin` items, and the
best plays.  Probe: a-c1-s0001 picks soft-at-25 (23 items, 14 soft) in
111 s, a-c1s-s0006 count-always in 109 s, a-c2-s0012 keeps the
planner's plan (49 items, 12 of 22 soft; the base dry-run's 57 items
hold few soft) in 147 s.  Two things the suite has to settle: the
replay margin (the planner's 22-item plan on a-c1s-s0006 replayed to
24 with 9 soft, and a margin of 2 gated it out for a 24-item plan with
4 soft), and whether the many-soft plans replay as well as the
hard-first ones (`psearch-core-a` at margin 2, `psearch1-core-a` at
margin 1, against `v34-core-a`).

The suite answered both.  Priced per episode at the platform's
weights with a load term (0.287 a fill point, 14.1 and 14.3 a unit of
soft and priority share, the gate at the threshold, and -1.7 points
per 0.01 of the centre-of-mass ratio), by the plan the search chose
(`reports/bench/v34-vs-psearch-core-a.txt`):

| variant chosen | episodes (margin 1) | priced vs v34 | without the load term | what it did |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| soft-at-25 | 10 | +100 | +100 | soft 5-7 -> 13-15 of 15, 4-7 fewer hard boxes, the load as low or lower, replay loss 0.8 a plan |
| soft-at-50 | 1 | +9 | +14 | soft 8 -> 11 |
| small-hard-first | 3 | -81 | -23 | soft 5 -> 0, the load 0.10 higher |
| count-always | 2 | -13 | +4 | soft 5 -> 3, the load 0.10 higher |
| soft-split | 1 | -27 | -20 | 23 planned, 19 placed, under the threshold |
| planner (unchanged) | 31 | -2 | -2 | |

So the gain is the soft position and nothing else: the hard-heavy
variants win the gate with their count and lose at the prices, having
no soft and a taller load, which the value does not see.  At margin 2
the planner's 22-item plans were gated out for 23-item dry-run plans
that replayed to 19 (five crossings lost of 48, -0.77 on the total);
at margin 1 one crossing was lost and the suite came out level (+0.04)
only because the hard-heavy variants ate the soft ones' gain.  The
list is now the soft positions alone (`soft-at-25;soft-at-50`, margin
1; `psearch2-core-a` adds soft-at-35), expected at about +2 points an
episode on A, +0.7 on the total.

With the soft positions alone (`psearch2-core-a`): soft +1.63 a scene
(share 0.45 -> 0.53), priority level, placed -1.3, CoM +0.009, 47 of
48 over the threshold, priced -0.02 on the total -- level again, and
again for a reason the per-episode table shows.  soft-at-25 won nine
episodes for +96; but the base dry-run plan beat the planner's on
value in four (a-c1-s0002, s0009, s0012, c1s-s0010) for -99: there the
planner's plan sat at the threshold (21 of 41), the gate priced its
soft and priority at nothing, and the dry-run's 24-item plan with no
priority box and the load 0.1 higher took the episode, where under
v34's rule the planner's 21 replayed to 22 and counted.  And one
soft-at-50 plan of 22 replayed to 20 (-26).  So two rules: the base
choice keeps v34's logic (the planner's plan unless it is under the
threshold and the dry-run's is not), and a variant replaces it only
when it is worth more *and* holds the threshold plus two.  On the
`psearch2` record those rules would have kept six soft-at-25 wins
(+64) and none of the losses: about +1.3 an episode on A.

With those rules (`psearch3-core-a`, soft-at-25/35/50, margin 2):
soft +1.04 a scene (share 0.45 -> 0.52), priority +0.15, placed
-0.58, fill -0.09, CoM level, 48 of 48 over the threshold, priced
+0.47 on the total; soft-at-25 played six episodes, soft-at-35 one,
the planner's plan forty.  The suite's answer is positive.  The
platform's answer to the base it stands on is not: v34 (the planner's
plan preferred, the search's base rule) scored 50.65 against v32's
52.71, with fill, cog, stability and placement down together and the
fraction placed 0.6148 -> 0.6023 -- the threshold's signature, where
the suite had 48 of 48.  The planner's plans hold 22-27 items on 41
and the dry-run's 26-31; the platform's Task A episodes evidently sit
closer to the threshold than the suite's (its streams or its slower
replay), and smaller plans lose crossings there that the suite never
shows.  The soft-at-25 plans are smaller still (22-24).  So the plan
search is held: it stands on a base the platform rejected, and its
gain is a trade of count for soft that the platform prices against
us at the margin.  What it needs before another try is a count margin
read off the platform, not the suite: v32's form back on A first
(v36), then the search with its gate set well above the threshold
(plans of 26 or more on 41), measured on the platform as its own
single change.

v34 carried a second change, the dry-run's scratch agent with the
manifest, and the official loss had to be split between the two
before v36 could carry the fix.  The fix changes the scratch's count
threshold from 41 items a container to the manifest's total, so on a
manifest of another size the dry-run runs its count pass to a
different count; on 41 items (the suite, the sample) it is inert.
Measured on c1 manifests of 30 and 55 items, three seeds each, v32's
rule, physics replay (`scripts/manifest_probe.py`): all twelve episodes
identical with and without the fix, to the item, the soft and priority
counts, the fill, the centre of mass and the optimize second -- the
planner's plan won the race on every one, so the dry-run's plan, the
only thing the fix can change, never played.  The loss is the
preference's.  One more thing the probe shows: on 55 items the agent
places 25-28 against a threshold of 28, so a platform whose Task A
manifests are larger than the sample's sits at the margin on every
episode, which is where the preference's smaller plans would lose
crossings.

v36 (v32's rule on A, the veto on C, the scratch with the manifest)
then scored 52.51, level with v32 (placement -0.7, soft -0.4, the rest
flat), where the suite had the veto worth +2.0 items a scene on C.
The figure that moved was the optimize time: 136.7 s against v32's
112.9 and v33's 116.6, and v34's 136.5 -- the two builds with the
manifest in the scratch took 24 s longer on the platform's longest
Task A optimize.  The manifest sets the dry-run's count threshold
from the manifest's total instead of 41 a container, and on a
two-container manifest of 110 items (threshold 56 against 42) the
probe reproduces it: seed 1's optimize 113 s -> 149 s with the
manifest's total (the plan the same, the planner's), seed 2 level.
The single-container probes never showed it because the ladder
declines above both thresholds there.  So on the platform's Task A
manifests, which are larger than the sample's 41, the dry-run runs its
count pass to a higher count -- the cover-allowed physics resort
before every decline: 24 s more, and plans with covered boxes when
they win the race, the placement and soft dips of v36 and a share of
v34's loss.  v37 keeps the scratch's Task A exemptions (the veto stays
C's) and goes back to the 41-based threshold
(`scratch_manifest_total` off): v32 plus the veto on C alone.

### After v33: the optimize race, and two probes that did nothing

Task A's optimize runs the row planner and then the ladder's dry-run
with what is left of the budget, and the plan with the higher score
(fill, soft share, priority share, count) plays.  Two runs of the same
arm on the A suite (`v33-core-a`, `strictfirst-core-a`) differed by
soft +0.9 a scene and fill -1.0 on the thirteen scenes where a
different plan won; what wins depends on how far the dry-run gets in
the time, which differs by machine.  `plan_prefer_planner`: the
planner's plan whenever it exists.  On the suite (`prefplan-core-a`
against `v33-core-a`) soft +1.35 a scene (12 scenes up, 1 down),
priority +0.42, fill -1.64, CoM -0.019, topples 6 -> 8 -- the same
figures as the run where the planner's plan won the race by itself,
and now the same on every machine.  One crossing lost (46 -> 47 of 48
against 48): a-c1s-s0010, where the planner's plan holds 17 of 41 (the
planner done in 16 s, the shelf scene's rows short) against the
dry-run's 22, and the plain preference played it to 20 placed against
22.  The preference now stops at the count threshold: a planner plan
with fewer entries than the threshold yields to a dry-run plan that
reaches it, and s0010 replays to the dry-run's 22 again.

`pocket_guard_hard` (the pocket guard for small hard floor picks, v33
C arm, `pockethard-core`): the guard never fired on 48 episodes (no
"pocket-hard" alternative in any record) -- the ladder rarely puts a
hard box under 0.2 m^2 on the floor by itself.  The run is therefore
a second sample of the v33 C arm, and gives the suite's run-to-run
noise: placed -0.19 a scene (4 up, 5 down, c-c2-s0005 -7, c-c2-s0008
+4), fill -0.14, CoM -0.002, 48 of 48 over the threshold both times.
A Task C change under 0.2 items a scene is inside that noise.

`soft_first_mode=tops` (Task B, the soft-first rule without the shelf
gallery: a soft box goes first only onto soft cargo or a top too high
for any hard box; `softtops-core-b` against `v33-core-b`): soft -0.17
a scene (4 up, 9 down), priority -0.08, priority covered 8 -> 11, fill
+0.05.  Negative; B's soft rule stays off.

`shadow_shake` on the v33 C arm (`shake33-core`; the tilted-gravity
shake in the shadow for poses with the bottom at 0.5 m or standing,
vetoing a pose under which any box shifts 0.1 m or tips 30 degrees):
2386 poses shaken, 462 vetoed, and the suite lost 0.92 items a scene
(6 up, 18 down), three crossings (48 -> 45 of 48), fill -0.67, with the
slowest call 0.9 s later and topples 9 -> 8.  On the v29 base the
shake was level (placed +0.15, topples 8 against 7); with the count
mode's one pass and the shade veto in the same window, its cost in
time and in refused poses is now count, and count is what the
threshold prices.  Not adopted.

### The wider ladder: a negative result

With the validator 4.7x faster the ladder can afford more candidates.
Denser anchors (`max_anchor_x` 26 -> 40, `max_anchor_y` 22 -> 34,
`max_candidates_per_orientation` 220 -> 400, `shortlist_size` 100) on
the analytic B suite against the same agent with the default width:
placed -0.19 a scene (6 better, 8 worse), fill -0.36, priority +0.06,
tail 5.66 s against 5.78 s.  The ladder is not candidate-starved; its
losses are the mirror's and the order's, not the grid's.  Six
orientations instead of three (`ladder_orientations` = 6): running.

## Executor status

| region | plain PPO vs best hand rule | soft-taught PPO | soft-taught + look-ahead | physics acceptance | beam ceiling (6 streams) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| wedge (c1) | 0.068 vs 0.050 m^3 | 0.077 | 0.087 (horizon 8, 6 s deadline) | 203/203 plain; 38/40 streams soft-taught | 0.084 |
| shelf (c1s) | 0.438 vs 0.378 m^3 | 0.445 | 0.491 (6 s deadline) | 279/279 plain; 40/40 soft-taught | 0.467 |
| stack (c1, ladder's board) | 0.282 vs 0.238 m^3 (0.309 after a continuation) | (not yet) | 0.421 (6 s deadline) | 26/40 episodes clean (90 % of stacked boxes); 19/40 with look-ahead; realised 0.215 whatever the policy | 0.407 |

The wedge and shelf executors stand at 80-85 % of their action-space
ceiling after plain PPO and reach it with the soft-target round plus
look-ahead.  The stack executor is the newest and the one with the
largest gap to its ceiling (0.282 against 0.407) and the largest gain
from look-ahead (+0.113); its next steps are the same recipe (soft
targets from the look-ahead's child values, then retrain) and a validator
that is not optimistic about tall stacks.  With three executors that
each cover one region of the container, the manager (`docs/rl-roadmap.md`)
has something to route to.

## Caveats

* The environment is a strip in an otherwise empty container.  In an episode
  the boxes passed on go elsewhere, and neighbours to the right of the strip
  may constrain the base.  The PASS action makes this composable, but the
  volume figure is for the isolated strip.
* The evaluation container is c1 (no main shelf); c1s has the same chamfer
  but the shelf changes what can stand at the floor line.
* One seed, one layout.  The continuation run (dispatched from the
  iteration-180 checkpoint) and a c1s run are the next data points.

## Reproduce

```
python -m wedge_rl baselines --layout c1 --episodes 40 --seed0 20000
python -m wedge_rl train --layout c1 --iterations 400 --episodes 32 --out runs/wedge
python -m wedge_rl eval --policy reports/wedge/ppo-c1-s0 --episodes 40 --seed0 20000
python -m wedge_rl replay --policies ppo,staircase --policy reports/wedge/ppo-c1-s0 \
    --episodes 40 --seed0 20000 --out runs/replay.jsonl --summary runs/replay-summary.json
```

Long runs go through `.github/workflows/wedge-ppo.yml` (workflow_dispatch; a
run can start from a previous run's checkpoint via `init_run_id`).

## **Sub-agent B/C: the grid rescue's window on Task B (`grid_rescue_pool_seconds`)**

Diagnosis (hard2 sample stratum, margin -6..-1; `last_call_probe.py`, arms with `deterministic_budgets=true`):

* C (11 declines probed: s4, s6, s7, s13, s14, s26, s27, s28, s39, s40, s48): 9 have no validator-legal pose for the stopping item anywhere (grid scan at 2 cm: 0 poses; verdicts mostly `settled-pose-outside`, i.e. no footprint fits under the ceiling on the tops; fill at the end 25-37 %). 2 have legal poses that were not played: s6 (108 grid poses, 1 shadow check before the cap), s27 (28 poses at 1.24 m, no check). Cause 1 = layout fragmentation (not a late-episode fix); cause 2 = small.
* B (5 declines probed: s6, s7, s14, s21, s39): s14 and s21 had legal grid poses (62 in c1; 480 + 361 in c0) and the grid rescue ran 0 checks. Mechanism: on B the resort runs to budget + 3.0 s (count pass, `count_mode_extra_seconds_pool`) or + 2.5 s, past the grid rescue's cap (budget + `grid_rescue_cap` = 6.5 s, sized for the 8 s limit of A/C), so the grid rescue's deadline is already behind it. s21 traced: resort ended at 6.59 s, grid deadline capped at 6.5 s, 0 tries. 32/96 hard2-b and 14/48 core-b episodes end on a call over 6.4 s (`grid2-*` records). The agent's count threshold (41 items/container) is under the hard2 streams' counts (s21: reads 42, actual 51), so `_really_under` is false there.

Change: `grid_rescue_pool_seconds` (off by default). With a pool (len > 1), the physics resort's deadline is moved this many seconds earlier and the grid rescue runs to the resort's old deadline without its own window or cap; when the resort overruns, the grid gets at least 1 s from its start, at most 0.5 s past the resort's old deadline. Task A/C (one item) untouched (the code path is identical when pool == 1). Arm: B baseline + `,grid_rescue_pool_seconds=1.5`. Branch `claude/rl-packing-policy-ftogt3-bc`.

Local trace b-hard2-s0021 (det): 48 -> 50 (grid rescue placed at two calls that the resort left empty), still -1.

hard2-b, time-faithful (judge2, crossings by stratum; records `reports/bench/sub-*`):

| run | sample | stretch | edge | fill-only | total | placed sample / stretch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| base r1 (`grid2-hard2-b`) | 29 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 38 | 40.90 / 46.31 |
| base r2 (`sub-bcbase-hard2-b-r2`) | 30 | 7 | 0 | 0 | 37 | 40.54 / 45.34 |
| new r1 (`sub-gpool-hard2-b`) | 31 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 40 | 41.21 / 47.16 |
| new r2 (`sub-gpool-hard2-b-r2`) | 32 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 41 | 41.60 / 47.81 |

* Mean +3.0 crossings (sample +2.0, stretch +1.0). Crossed in both new runs and neither base run: s0031, s0042 (both base ends were `fallback-after-veto` placements that failed transport/settle). Crossed in both base runs and neither new: s0036 (base 84 and 83 against threshold 83, so margin +1/0; new 72, 75; first divergence at step 3/8 on pocket-guard timing). Flaky between base runs: s1, s23, s47, s53, s54; between new runs: s40.
* Policy time: s0091 (fill-only) is over 10 s in every arm (base 18.05 / 17.50, new 14.16 / 17.18); every other episode max is 7.8 s new vs 8.1 s base. No new violation.
* Deterministic pair (`sub-bcbase-det-hard2-b` vs `sub-gpool-det-hard2-b`): sample 27 -> 25, stretch 4 -> 5, placed sample 39.85 -> 40.15, stretch 43.69 -> 44.72. Not an attribution: `deterministic_budgets` leaves the resort's clock, the very thing this option changes (s0007: base resort hit at 4.23 s, new resort missed and the grid rescue placed at 6.92 s; s0031 diverged at a stack-rl call at 6.3 s), and the det B runs differ from the time-faithful ones by up to 85 items on one scene (s0091).

core-b, time-faithful (`sub-gpool-core-b` vs `grid2-core-b`): 48 -> 48 crossings; placed 39.02 -> 38.58 (6 up, 11 down); margin 0/+1: 2 -> 3 (b-c2-s0007 at 42/42); policy time max 7.40 s (base 7.34).
core-b replicate (`sub-gpool-core-b-r2`): 48 -> 48; placed 39.02 -> 38.94 (7 up, 10 down); margin 0/+1: 2 -> 2; policy time max 7.35 s. Core C / hard2 C not rerun: with one item in the pool the option does not act and the code path is the baseline's.

Recommendation: ship on B (arm `arm_grid2_b` + `,grid_rescue_pool_seconds=1.5`): +3 hard2-b crossings on the mean of two time-faithful pairs, no core-b crossing lost in two runs, no new policy-time violation. Not attributed by a noise-free pair (see above); the two gains are the episodes whose base ends were failed fallback placements, where the grid rescue now gets a turn first.

## **Sub-agent B/C round 2: the sealing design gated to the low phase (`rescue_order_gate`)**

Change: `rescue_order_gate` (default "" = the old semantics: `rescue_deep_first` / `rescue_seal_area` act in every rescue call). A "+"-joined set of conditions that must all hold at the call: `low` (a container's usable surface under `phase_switch_surface`, the phase switch's own reading; read by the gate also where the phase switch skipped it, an exhausted Task B pool), `count` (the count pass and the grid rescue after it), `under` (the agent's count estimate at least `rescue_order_margin` items under the threshold). Gate chosen: `low`. Why: `count` is open from step 1 on C (under the agent's 41-a-container estimate every call is a count pass until the threshold), and `under` alone likewise; only the low phase is late by construction (it persists once reached). On core C the low phase begins at 55-75 % of the episode (`phase.low_steps` against the steps). Arm: v47 form + `,rescue_deep_first=true,rescue_seal_area=0.2,rescue_order_gate=low` (B: on top of `grid_rescue_pool_seconds=1.5`).

Deterministic pair, hard2 C (`grid2det-hard2` vs `sub2-lowdet-hard2`):

| stratum | crossings | placed | margin 0/+1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| sample | 33 -> 36 | 41.56 -> 43.58 (+2.02; 17 up, 9 down) | 5 -> 6 |
| stretch | 5 -> 5 | 45.53 -> 46.81 (+1.28) | 2 -> 3 |
| edge / fill-only | 0 -> 0 | -2.38 / +0.12 | |

Divergence (first step where the episodes differ, against the first low-phase step): 76 of 96 episodes change, 69 at or after the low phase (gate), 7 before it (the residual time noise `deterministic_budgets` leaves, e.g. s0036 stack-rl vs pocket-soft at a 4.3 s ladder call). Gate-attributed: gained s0004 (-4 -> 0), s0007 (-1 -> +2), s0026 (-1 -> 0), s0048 (-3 -> +1); lost s0010 (+3 -> -1, diverges at 30/48 in a count-mode call). The ungated design on the same pair (`sealdet-hard2`): gained s0004, s0007, s0026, s0027; lost s0041 (diverges at step 13/73), s0044 (15/37), s0054 (37/67) -- two of its three losses were early divergences the gate removes.

Time-faithful (judge2; B judged against the v47 B form):

| suite | base | crossings | placed | margin 0/+1 | policy max (new) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| hard2 C | `grid2-hard2` | 36 -> 42 (sample 31 -> 38, stretch 5 -> 4) | +1.96 / +0.69 (sample / stretch) | 8 -> 9 | 16.4 s s0091 (base 16.0), next 6.2 s |
| hard2 B | `sub-gpool-hard2-b` / `-r2` | 40 -> 40 / 41 -> 40 (sample 31, stretch 9) | +0.00 / -0.40 sample | 6 -> 9 / 8 -> 9 | 15.1 s s0091 (base 14.2 / 17.2), next 8.2 s (s0061) |
| hard C | `grid2-hard` | 19 -> 26 | 42.42 -> 44.73 (+2.31; 26 up, 6 down) | 2 -> 5 | 6.7 s |
| hard B | `sub2-base-hard-b` (v47 B, this round) | 19 -> 22 | 41.81 -> 43.52 (+1.71; 18 up, 11 down) | 3 -> 3 | 7.4 s |
| core C | `grid2-core` | 48 -> 48 | 36.88 -> 38.00 (+1.12; 23 up, 8 down) | 6 -> 5 | 5.8 s (base 6.3) |
| core B | `sub-gpool-core-b` | 48 -> 47 | 38.58 -> 38.71 (+0.12) | 3 -> 4 | 7.4 s |
| core B r2 | `sub-gpool-core-b` / `-r2` | 48 -> 48 / 48 -> 48 | +0.65 / +0.29 | 3 -> 2 / 2 -> 2 | 7.4 s |

(`sub2-base-hard-b` against `grid2-hard-b`: 19 -> 19, so hard B's +3 is the gate's arm, not the pool window.)  Ungated on the same C baselines: hard2 C 36 -> 39 (`seal-hard2`), core C +1.25.

* core B's one loss (run 1): b-c2-s0003 +7 -> -6, diverging at step 19 of 50 (pocket-soft vs sp-cluster at a 1.0-1.4 s ladder call; the two baseline runs themselves differ at step 20), before any rescue (first gated call at step 32): time noise, not the gate. The replicate keeps 48 of 48. Core C's near-threshold episodes (base margin 0: c1-s0003, c1-s0010, c1s-s0003, c1s-s0004) end at 0, +1, 0, 0.
* Policy time: no new violation (s0091 over in every arm; b-hard2-s0061 8.2 s is under B's 10 s).
* B: the hard2 B pair is flat (0 / -1 crossings, inside the base runs' own 37-41 spread); hard B +3.

Recommendation: ship on C (`arm_grid2_c` + `,rescue_deep_first=true,rescue_seal_area=0.2,rescue_order_gate=low`): +6 hard2 C, +7 hard C, core C 48/48 with +1.12 placed, deterministic pair +3 (gate-attributed +4 / -1). On B it is optional: hard B +3, hard2 B flat, core B 48/48 in the replicate, one time-noise loss in run 1; ship on B only if the platform's B is judged like C (the gate acts on B too, 2-50 calls an episode). Residual risk: the gate still changes the course of 69 of 96 hard2 C episodes and 37 of 48 core C episodes from the low phase on, so a platform edge episode whose last items come in the low phase can still move either way; what the gate removes is the reordering of the early rescue placements (the v44 failure mode, s0041/s0044 here).

## **Phenomenon 13: the simulator's height rules at the shelf level (`band_lift`)**

The question: why does the real simulator refuse (`is_valid` False, end reason `transport`) a pose the shadow passed, on 3-4 of 96 hard2 C episodes and on one hard B scene in every record set?

Cause. `check_transport_path` lifts the box 8 cm (`start_z`) before carrying it in from the door, with two exceptions, both read on the *commanded* pose: (1) the zero-lift band -- when the bottom is within 5 cm above a resting surface the box slides in at its own height; the resting surfaces are the floor and the shelf level `height/2 + thickness + buffer`, tested in every container, shelf or not; (2) the ceiling clip -- when the top is within `start_z + 0.018` under a ceiling surface the lift is cut to the clearance less 0.0185; the ceiling surfaces are the real ceiling and the shelf level's underside `height/2 + buffer`, again in every container. A box resting on a packed item in either band is carried in at (or within 1.5 cm of) its support, `getClosestPoints(distance=0.015)` reports the support, and the box is refused. The agent's analytic sweep models both rules and vetoes those poses itself (`transport-hits-packed-item`), so the two bands were dead for stacking on items in every build so far; the floor and the shelf top have their own escapes (`FLOOR_ACTION_LIFT` 2 cm inside the floor band, `SHELF_ACTION_LIFT` 5.1 cm = 1 mm over the shelf band).

The leak is the edges. The platform evaluates the rules in float32 (`place_pos` is a float32 array; NumPy 2 keeps `float32 - float` in float32 -- the Dockerfile's Miniconda py312 with `pip install gymnasium pybullet` lands on NumPy 2; under NumPy 1.26 the same expression is float64 and the rules agree with the agent's). The agent's float64 `bottom - level` for a bottom exactly at `level + 0.05` is `0.0500000045` (outside the band), the simulator's float32 is `0.04999995` (inside); a top exactly at `height/2` is `clearance = 0` there (clip to zero lift) and `-1e-17` here (no clip). Every transport ending in the records sits on one of these edges to the millimetre (survey of all `reports/bench/*` records): bottom = level + 0.05 (c-hard-s0004 step 43, c-hard2-s0070 step 55, b-hard-s0001 step 17 of 61 in every hard-b record set, b-hard2-s0047 one short of its threshold, b-hard2-s0031 one short, c-hard2-s0047/s0048), bottom = level (c-hard2-s0041, c-hard2-s0022, b-hard-s0013, c-hard-s0033, b-c1s-s0012 and the other core `0.86` endings), top = height/2 (a-hard-s0008 step 25 of 30 in ten A record sets, c-hard2-s0091 in five, c-hard2-s0031, c-hard2-s0049). `rule_alpha/transport.py` guards the band's edges with 3 mm of slack (the v6 fix, eight of 48 episodes then) but only on the `conservative_transport` path, which the shipped form turned off at v5; the fast path, the production samples and the shadow's own mirror (`shadow.py`, float64 on the agent's pose) have no guard.

Official simulator, `scripts/band_probe.py` (1.61 m ULD, no shelf; level 0.845, underside 0.805; a 0.3 m box on a column):

| bottom or top of the box | unlifted | commanded by `band_lift` |
|---|---|---|
| bottom = level + 0.005 / + 0.025 / + 0.05 / + 0.0 | refused, collision with the support at 7.7 mm | placed, settled on the support |
| bottom = level - 0.01 / + 0.06 | placed | placed (unchanged command) |
| top = underside / - 0.02 / - 0.03 | refused | placed (top commanded 3 mm over the underside) |
| top = underside - 0.05 / + 0.01 | placed | placed (unchanged) |

Change: `band_lift` (metres, off by default; 0.003 measured). When the simulator applies no lift of its own (not a floor or shelf contact): a pose whose bottom lies in the band, 3 mm of slack at both edges, is commanded with its bottom `band_lift` above the band's top (level + 0.053), the shelf lift's own escape; in a container without a shelf, a pose whose top lies within 0.0185 + 0.016 under the level's underside (3 mm of slack) is commanded with its top `band_lift` over it, so the clip does not fire. Under a real shelf the top rule has no escape and the pose stays refused. The sweep and the inclusion are modelled from the commanded pose in both validator paths (`fastgeom.band_lift_z`, `_swept_box`); the shadow receives the commanded pose and mirrors the simulator from there. The box drops up to 5.6 cm onto its support at the place (the shelf's cargo already drops 5.1 cm). Census of the records' final boards (`sub2-low-*`, `adrop-*`): 4.5-4.9 % of the free tops on hard2 C/B lie in the bottom band (53 of 96 episodes have one), 2.7-4.3 % on core C/B, 8.4 % on hard A -- surfaces no build could stack on.

Arms: C `sub2-low-hard2` + `,band_lift=0.003`; B `sub2-low-hard2-b` + the same; A `arm_adrop` (v46 A form) + the same. Records `reports/bench/band-*`.

## **Phenomenon 14: how far ahead is the miss visible?**

The question: the checkmate guard found every alternative dead when it fired, and the learned gate read the scene's size; is there a signal earlier than the last call?  Read from the records (`sub2-low-hard2`, the v48 C form; the usable surface, `surface.usable_surface_ratio` with the 0.40 x 0.55 x 0.24 window, rebuilt after every placement from the settled poses; `scratchpad/surface_traj.py`, `killer.py`, `waste.py`, `foresee.py`).

* The collapse is visible long before the end. In the 54 episodes under their threshold the low phase (ratio under 0.2) begins a median 14 placements before the end (quartiles 7-35), and the largest one-placement drop of the ratio (median 0.22 of the floor area, three times a box's footprint) sits a median 15 placements before the end; 51 of 54 episodes have a drop over 0.1 with more than five placements left. The dropping placement is a floor placement in every case listed: the ladder's max-footprint rung (13 of 54), the soft edge (11), the physics resort (7), terrace-extension and stack-rl (6 each); 54 % at the door side (y < -0.2), 19 % standing. Per placement the mean drop is 0.045 (max-footprint), 0.046 (soft-edge), 0.019 (physics-resort), 0.012 (terrace-extension).
* The drop does not tell the misses from the crossings. Within the sample stratum the crossing episodes waste more usable surface than the missing ones in their first half (median 2.14 vs 1.38 m2 beyond the boxes' own footprints; the largest single waste 0.93 vs 0.68 m2): the crossings simply play more placements. The ratio k items before the threshold, among the episodes that got that far, separates the two with AUC 0.61 at k = 5, 0.70 at k = 8, 0.72 at k = 12, and inverts at k = 1-3 (the crossings have spent their surface by then; the misses still hold 0.15 of the floor they cannot use for the item in hand). Five items before the threshold the board does not say which way the episode goes.
* What does tell is the demand, known at step 0: sample stratum 38 of 48 cross, stretch 4 of 32, edge and fill-only 0 of 16; within a stratum the demand alone has AUC 0.63 (sample) / 0.61 (stretch) for the miss on C, 0.79 / 0.63 on B.
* The one lever this points at was tried: the surface arbiter (findings, "The instrument as an arbiter") replaced the rung's pick by the candidate after which the most usable surface is kept, 64 overrides in 48 sample C episodes, placed -0.42, 48 -> 47 crossings; the shade veto (`shade_veto_area`, in the shipped form) refuses the poses that wall off the floor from the sweep. The drops above happen with the shade veto on.

So the miss is decided by the volume the first threshold items demand, not by a placement the board shows coming: the killing placement is identifiable in hindsight (a floor placement that fragments the plateau a dozen items before the end), but the same placements occur in the crossing episodes, and choosing against them by the surface they keep (the arbiter) cost placements. Nothing further on this line.
