## **The future selector: the ladder's own rollouts as the judge of its choices**

The one floor-formation route left open after the levelness line (theory, sections 6 and 10): among the ladder's survivors, apply each to the board and let the ladder itself pack sampled continuations of the stream from there, until it declines; take the candidate after which the most items stand. The v18 rollout selector lost with a heightmap packer as the judge (it fits 15-17 of 41 on an empty floor against the ladder's 19-27); here the judge is the ladder (`rule_alpha/foresight.py`, `config.future_selector`; the futures from the SKU classes seen so far, the sample's mix as the prior; the rollouts apply and withdraw their placements on the live board). Measured offline on the analytic model with the ladder alone (`arm_light_c`: the v49 C form without the shadow, the resort, the grid rescue, the pocket guard, the count mode and the phase switch, deterministic budgets), where a decision costs 0.2 s and a selection 5-10 s; records `reports/bench/*-an`.

| variant | core C placed / crossings | hard2 C sample | hard2 C stretch | overrides a scene | minutes a scene |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| light arm (the judge and the player) | 28.10 / 10 of 48 | 32.54 / 13 of 48 | 37.34 / 1 of 32 | | 0.1 |
| every decision, first 3 survivors, 2 futures | +0.50 (20 up, 17 down) / 10 | +0.83 / 14 | -0.66 / 1 | 4.6 of 26 | 7-12 |
| floor placements only, 4 survivors, 6 futures | +0.23 / 11 | +0.08 / 12 | -0.25 / 1 | 2.1 of 26 | 11-16 |
| one candidate per rung, 4 rungs, 3 futures | +0.17 (17 up, 17 down) / 9 | +0.46 / 13 | +0.84 / 3 | 2.7 of 26 | 6-8 |

Verdict: under one item a scene in every form, inside the up/down noise, with the crossings moving by one either way; the one-per-rung form is the only one with a sign on hard2 (+0.46 sample, +0.84 stretch, two stretch crossings), still within what two runs of the same arm differ by. With the ladder as the judge the rollouts say that its survivors -- near-twins of one rung or the best of each rung -- lead to the same count a dozen items on; the choice among them is not where the items are, which is what the levelness line found with one-step measures and the pairs study found with the true continuations (AUC 0.64-0.68). A foresight that could add several items would have to generate poses the ladder does not (its rungs are the candidate set here), i.e. a different packer, not a selector. At 6-16 minutes a scene offline it is also two orders over the online budget. Closed.

**v49 on the platform**: 55.62, the best so far (v46 55.34, +0.28; v47 and v48 not scored). Soft +4.2, fill +0.51, cog -1.41, stability -0.78, placement unchanged, the placed fraction 0.6363 -> 0.6475; policy time 6.91 s. The band's placements are stacks on packed tops at 0.85-0.95 m, which is where the soft cargo goes, so the soft component moved most and the height components paid; the count rose by about one item in ninety. The A suite's priced reading of the same lever (cog -0.5 to -0.9) is what kept it off Task A; on B and C the count and the soft share outweigh it.

## **The covers of priority cargo over the threshold (`count_cover_margin`)**

The placement score on the platform (49.45) is the credited share of the priority cargo: on sample C the v49 form places 66 % of the stream's priority boxes and then covers 22 % of those with other cargo, net 51 %. The covers are the count pass's, and on the sample suites most happen after the episode is over its threshold (C: 35 of 58; B: 56 of 62; hard2 C: 20 of 91), where a covered priority box costs its share (about 2 points of the episode under the H1 pricing) and the box placed on it is worth its fill. `count_cover_margin` stops the other-attribute covers once the placed count reaches the agent's threshold estimate plus the margin (records `cov0-*`, `cov3-*`, against the v49 form):

| suite | margin | crossings | placed | priority covered | priced |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core C | 0 / 3 | 48 -> 47 / 47 (c-c2-s0003 45 -> 41 against 42, both) | -3.29 / -2.54 | 46 -> 18 / 26 | +0.54 / +0.22 |
| core B | 0 / 3 | 48 -> 48 / 48 | -2.79 / -2.12 | 43 -> 8 / 18 | -0.18 / -0.01 |
| hard2 C | 0 / 3 | sample 35 -> 32 / 33, stretch 11 -> 8 / 8 | sample -1.0 / -0.8 | 72 -> 30 / 38 | +0.24 / +0.15 |
| hard2 B | 0 / 3 | sample 35 -> 32 / 32, stretch 11 -> 11 | sample -2.0 / -1.4 | 89 -> 39 / 47 | -0.04 / -0.02 |

Not adopted. The covers it removes are real, but stopping them ends the episodes two to three items earlier (the count pass with the covers is what carries the end of a sample episode), the soft share falls with them on B (0.544 -> 0.435), and on hard2 the crossings fall on both tasks: the agent's threshold is a guess of 41 items a container, the hard2 streams carry more, and "over the estimate" is under the real threshold there. That is the platform's situation too (its counts are unknown), so the estimate, not the margin, is what this lever would need. The priority-only form (`count_cover_priority=false`, soft covers kept) is measured next.

Soft columns stand (`scripts/column_probe.py`, official simulator with the bench's shake proxy): a column of three or four soft D boxes (1.24 / 1.44 m) alone shifts 1.5 / 3.5 cm under the shake and nothing topples; braced by five-high hard columns on both sides it shifts more (5.9 / 14 cm, the thin hard flankers move) and still nothing topples. The 0.7 m cap on soft structure (v39) came from mixed stacks' topples, not from straight columns. A soft box slides about 1 cm after its placement, so a hard box set beside a soft column at the 2.6 cm design gap can be refused at the sweep (1.4 cm left).

**The priority-only cover stop** (`count_cover_priority=false`, soft covers kept; `ncp-*`): core C 48 -> 47, placed -0.69; core B 48 -> 48, placed -1.19 (soft share 0.544 -> 0.481); hard2 C 35/11 -> 35/11, placed -0.5 / -1.2; hard2 B sample 35 -> 34. Priced +0.25 / -0.08 / +0.08 / -0.11. Not adopted; with the margin form above, the covers line is closed: every way of refusing the covers ends the episodes earlier and the count carries more than the shares.

## **The settle endings: the shadow's wobble as a hard veto**

The settle endings (the box placed, then fallen past the simulator's 0.3 m / 45 deg at the settle) on the v49 records: hard2 B 10 of 96 (9 under their threshold), hard2 C 3, core 1 each. Three classes (`scratchpad` survey of every settle ending): (a) the agent's last-resort gambles where the shadow had already said the pose falls (`fallback-after-veto`, 4 of 16) -- no alternative existed; (b) the shadow saw a wobble, 3-8 cm of drift or 4-15 deg of tilt, under the hard-veto limits (`shadow_hard_drift_xy` 0.10 m, `shadow_hard_angle_deg` 15), kept the pose, and the real 300-step settle grew the wobble into a fall (6 of 16; e.g. b-hard2-s0037 step 9, drift 5 cm and 4 deg in the shadow, 57 cm and 44 deg in the simulator); (c) no shadow check, skipped for time on the big B boards (4 of 16). The wobble band holds 131 placed against 3 fallen on hard2 B, 160 against 2 on hard2 C.

Hard veto at 5 cm / 8 deg (`strict-*`; the replicates `-r2`):

| suite | crossings | placed | settle endings | shake topples | priced |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core C | 48 -> 48 | -0.52 | 1 -> 1 | 14 -> 14 | -0.14 |
| core B (two runs) | 48 -> 48 / 48 | -0.50 / -1.00 | 1 -> 1 | 29 -> 19 / 14 | -0.02 / +0.02 |
| hard2 C | sample 35 -> 35, stretch 11 -> 11 | +0.33 / -0.06 | 3 -> 2 | 74 -> 67 | +0.06 |
| hard2 B (two runs) | sample 35 -> 39 / 35, stretch 11 -> 10 / 11 | +0.56 / +0.35 sample | 10 -> 6 / 5 | 105 -> 69 / 92 | -0.28 / -0.23 |

Not adopted. The settle endings halve on hard2 B and the shake topples fall by a third, but the crossings do not follow (the +4 of the first B run is a one-run swing: the v49 arm's own replicates move a stratum by one, and the second run is flat), and the refused wobbles cost half an item a scene on the sample suites with the priced reading at -0.1 to -0.3; the platform's stability component did not move with the bench's topples before (v46: 7 -> 12 topples, 79.23 -> 79.26). The settle endings that remain are the gambles (a) and the skipped checks (c).

**The own-kind stacking selector** (`attribute_stack_selector`, `room.AttributeStackSelector`; `own-*`): a priority or soft item goes on its own kind when a survivor stands there. It never fired (0 overrides in 192 episodes): the ladder offers a soft item no pose on a soft top until soft covers 35 % of the floor (`soft_structure_floor_share`), and the priority survivors on tops stood on hard cargo in every call probed (`scratchpad` spy on C:c1:1 and C:c2:3: 0 of 55 soft survivors on an item, 0 of 13 priority survivors on priority). A selector cannot choose what the generators do not offer; the records are replicates of the v49 arm (hard2 C sample 35 -> 34, B 35 -> 34: the arm's own noise is a crossing a stratum). The zone has to be opened at the generator: soft as structure from the first soft box and the cap raised to 1.2 m (the column probe above), with the selector to prefer it (`zone-*`, measured next).

**The zone form** (`zone-*`: soft as structure from the first soft box, `soft_structure_floor_share=0`, the cap raised to 1.2 m, `soft_structure_max_top=1.2`, with the own-kind selector; against the v49 form; `bandntdet-hard2` / `zonedet-hard2` the deterministic hard2 C pair):

| suite | crossings | placed | soft covered | settle endings | priced |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core C | 48 -> 48 | +0.96 (22 up, 10 down) | 45 -> 25 | 1 -> 1 | +0.08 |
| core B (two runs) | 48 -> 47 / 48 (b-c2-s0003, the flaky one: 37 / 50) | +0.38 / +0.88 | | | +0.27 / +0.11 |
| hard2 C | sample 35 -> 34, stretch 11 -> 10 | +1.31 / +0.50 | 73 -> 65 | 3 -> 5 | -0.22 |
| hard2 B | sample 35 -> 36, stretch 11 -> 12 | +1.48 / +3.41 (17 up, 3 down) | | 10 -> 13 | -0.43 (cog -0.75) |
| hard2 C deterministic | sample 36 -> 36 (4 in, 4 out), stretch 11 -> 8 | -1.60 / +1.06 | 59 -> 47 | 1 -> 6 | -0.30 |

The selector never fires here either (the rungs put soft on soft once it is structure; the pick needs no override). The floor is freed and the sample suites gain a placement a scene with every crossing kept, but the 1.2 m columns pay at the height: the deterministic pair loses three stretch crossings and five episodes end at the settle (soft columns that the shadow passes and the simulator drops, the class (b) of the settle study), and the cog reads -0.3 to -0.75 on hard2. The gate alone (soft as structure from the first box, the cap kept at 0.7 m; `gate0-*`) is measured next to separate the floor's gain from the height's cost.
## **The count threshold's estimate on B and C**

The question: on Tasks B and C the agent does not know the stream's length, so the count threshold (the platform scores cog, stability, placement and soft only when more than half of the stream is placed, `total // 2 + 1`) is a guess: `count_mode_items_per_container` (41) times the containers, strict (21 for one ULD, 42 for two, 62 for three). How wrong is it, what does it change, and can the agent do better from what it observes?

**What is observable** (`simulator/src/ground_handling/env.py`, `items.py`, `app.py`). `get_init_states` hands `optimize`, `lookahead_k` and `container_list` (dimensions, preloaded `packed_items`); each `policy` observation hands `container_list` (with every packed item's dimensions and `index`), `pool_list` (the visible items with `index`, dimensions, mass, flags) and `lookahead_k`. The total (`stream_manager.total_items`) is never handed over; only Task A's `optimize(item_list)` sees the manifest. The indices are the stream's positions (0..N-1 in all three official sample tasks and in the bench), so the largest index seen + 1 is the number of items seen so far -- the same number the agent can count, a lower bound on the total and nothing more: with the stream continuing, "total >= placed + pool" puts the threshold at most at (placed + pool) // 2 + 1, which is under the placed count from the first dozen items on and so never says "under". The stream's end is visible only on B (`max_space` 1 in both samples: the pool refills after every placement while the stream lasts, so a pool under `lookahead_k` means the stream is over and total = placed + pool), and only in its last `lookahead_k - 1` items, long after the threshold. On C (`look_ahead` 1) nothing is seen until the end. What else is observable is the volume: the containers' dimensions and the dimensions of every item seen.

**How wrong the guess is.** Official samples (`reports/submission/abc_config.json`): A 41 items (manifest known), B 42, C 42 in one ULD: real threshold 22, estimate 21. The bench's core suites carry exactly 41 a container (estimate exact on all 48 scenes of core C and core B). On hard2 the count is chosen for the demand of the first half (`make_hard2_scene`; not by the container's volume, which is the `hard` generator's rule) and the estimate is exact on 6 of 96 scenes, low on 67, high on 23 (estimate minus real: -234 .. +19, |error| 20.4 items a scene). Per-step on the v49 records (`scratchpad/est_err.py`; wrong-over = placed in [estimate, real), the agent reads itself over and is under; wrong-under the reverse):

| record | scenes est low / high / exact | placements wrong-over (of them rescue / covers) | wrong-under | episodes ending wrong-over |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `bandnt-core`, `bandnt-core-b` | 0 / 0 / 48 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `bandnt-hard2` | 67 / 23 / 6 | 906 (356 / 114) | 76 | 34 |
| `bandnt-hard2-b` | 67 / 23 / 6 | 750 (188 / 68) | 106 | 36 |
| `sub2-lowdet-hard2` | 67 / 23 / 6 | 836 (227 / 59) | 73 | 40 |

The episodes ending in the window include near misses (c-hard2-s0039, 42 items as on the sample: 21 placed against 22; s0065 85 / 86, s0027 26 / 29) and the whole of the stretch, edge and fill-only strata.

**What the error changes in v49: almost nothing.** Everything keyed off "under" sits in the rescue stages (the physics resort's count pass -- covering poses, support 0.25, the pool's smallest item first, `count_mode_extra_seconds` -- and the grid rescue's), and in v49 the phase switch makes them count passes in the low phase whatever the estimate says (`_under_count` returns True in the low phase; `phase_switch_cover=true` allows the covers there; `count_cover_priority` defaults on). Rebuilding the usable surface from the records' settled poses (`scratchpad/est_phase.py`): of the rescue placements taken in the wrong-over window, 322 of 356 on hard2 C and 146 of 188 on hard2 B are in the low phase; of the episodes declining in the window, 30 of 33 (C) and 23 of 30 (B) decline in it. In the shadow stats, 32 of the 34 hard2 C (and 33 of 36 hard2 B) episodes that end wrong-over ran the count pass at their end; only c-hard2-s0027 (never in the low phase: ratio min 0.26) declined after a resort without it. The estimate decides about 34 rescue placements and 3 declines on hard2 C, 42 and 7 on hard2 B (where an exhausted pool skips the phase reading), and nothing on core. Where it matters is in levers gated on it: `count_cover_margin` (the previous section) stops the covers over the estimate even in the low phase, and that is where the hard2 crossings went.

**Candidates** (replayed offline on the streams, `scratchpad/est_offline.py`: for each placed count the episode went through, the agent has seen the stream's first placed + pool items; strict thresholds):

| estimator | core C: mean abs error at the threshold / wrong-over / wrong-under steps | hard2 C: mean abs error / wrong-over / ending wrong-over | official B, C sample (real 22) | one ULD, 55 items of the core SKUs (real 28) | one ULD, 30 items (real 16) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| prior 41 (v49) | 0.00 / 0 / 0 | 20.4 / 940 / 34 | 21 (one short) | 21 | 21 |
| prior 42 | 1.00 / 0 / 48 | 19.9 / 871 / 32 | 22 | 22 | 22 |
| volume alone, 0.54 | 2.00 / 59 / 29 | 14.7 / 604 / 32 | 22 | 20.6 | 20.5 |
| max(prior 42, volume 0.54) | 1.40 / 0 / 62 | 13.8 / 339 / 20 | 22 | 22.1 | 22.2 |
| volume of the container x 42 | 0.75 / 36 / 0 | 18.7 / 758 / 34 | 21 | 20.5 | 20.5 |

The volume reading (`count_mode_stream_volume`): expected count = share x (sum of the containers' length x width x height) / (mean volume of every item seen so far, pool and packed); the share 0.54 is the official B and C samples' (42 items of 0.0629 m^3 mean in a 4.925 m^3 ULD: the stream is 0.536 of the volume; sample A 0.557). Alone it is noisy on the core streams (their stream volume varies with the SKU draw: 27 of 48 low, 59 wrong-over steps, all within five of the threshold), so it is taken only as the larger of the two (`max`), which can only lengthen the count pass: never wrong-over where the prior is right, wrong-over on hard2 cut from 940 to 339 decisions. What neither reading sees is a stream of the sample's boxes, only more of them (the 55-a-container row): the count is not observable there. The platform's placed fraction argues against that case: v49 places 0.6475 of the platform's streams against 0.61 / 0.62 / 0.60 of the samples' A / B / C (`official-sample-abc-v49.json`); a stream of the sample's boxes 1.3x longer would fill at the same volume and put the fraction near 0.47. The "estimate that grows" (total >= placed + pool) is the lower bound above and never says under; the conservative policy (keep the count pass past the estimate while the stream lasts) is a larger prior or `count_mode_margin`, measured below through prior 42 and the `max` form.

**Measured** (v49 forms `arm_bandnt_c` / `arm_bandnt_b` + the extra; records `reports/bench/est*`). The runners were 47 % slower than when the v49 records were made (core C mean policy call 0.76 s in `bandnt-core`, 1.12 s in the same form rerun now), and c-c2-s0003 (45 in `bandnt-core`, 41 against 42 in `cov0-core`, `cov3-core` and every run here) is lost by the v49 form itself on them, so every arm is judged against the v49 form rerun alongside (`estbase-*`), and two deterministic pairs (`estbase-det-*`, `deterministic_budgets=true`) were run.

| suite | v49 rerun (`estbase`) | prior 42 (`est42`) | max(42, volume 0.54) (`estv`) | `estv` + `count_cover_margin=0` (`estvc`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| core C crossings / placed / priced | 47 / 37.77 | 47 / +0.04 / +0.00 | 47 / +0.12 / -0.08 | 47 (c-c2-s0010 out, c-c2-s0003 in) / -2.83 / +0.37 |
| core B | 48 / 39.75 | 48 / -0.12 / -0.01 | 48 / -0.23 / -0.01 | 48 / -2.90 / +0.03 |
| hard2 C sample / stretch crossings | 33 / 11 | 33 / 11 | 32 / 10 | 33 / 8 |
| hard2 C placed / priced | 47.00 | -1.13 / -0.02 | -0.51 / -0.09 | -0.99 / +0.03 |
| hard2 B sample / stretch crossings | 33 / 12 | 34 / 11 | 35 / 10 | 33 / 11 |
| hard2 B placed / priced | 46.30 | -0.34 / +0.06 | +0.53 / +0.09 | -1.37 / +0.16 |
| det core C crossings / placed | 48 / 38.08 | 48 / -0.46 | 48 / -0.17 | |
| det hard2 C sample / stretch / placed | 37 / 11 / 47.17 | | 38 / 10 / -0.70 | |

Against the v49 records themselves (`bandnt-*`) the same arms read core C 48 -> 47 (c-c2-s0003, the runners), core B 48 -> 48, hard2 C sample 35 -> 33 / 32 / 33 and hard2 B 35 -> 34 / 35 / 33 -- the rerun of v49 itself reads 35 -> 33 on both. Policy time: no new episode over the limit (c-hard2-s0091 and s0070 / b-hard2-s0070 and s0091 are over it in the reruns of v49 too).

* Prior 42 moves almost nothing: on core C two scenes change against the rerun (both up), on the deterministic core pair the changes are the two-container scenes (threshold 43 against 42: c2-s0012 -6, c2-s0002 -5, c2p-s0003 -5; c1s-s0004 21 -> 26), no crossing moves.
* The volume form changes the hard2 courses at the rescue stages: in the deterministic pair 52 of 96 episodes diverge, 43 of them first at a resort or grid-rescue call; 29 of those 52 diverge before the old estimate was even reached, where the two forms behave alike, which is the residual noise `deterministic_budgets` leaves (findings, rescue_order_gate) -- the pair attributes nothing. Crossings in: s0006 (30 -> 35 against 34), s0010 (43 -> 47 / 44), s0040 (38 -> 42 / 42); out: s0043 (86 -> 80 / 85), s0048 (75 -> 61 / 64), s0059 (48 -> 46 / 48). Placed -0.70 on C, +0.53 on B: the count pass before the low phase (covering poses, 0.25 support, the extra second) is not free; it buys a crossing here and costs a dozen items there.
* With the better estimate the cover margin recovers one sample crossing on each task against `cov0` (C 32 -> 33, B 32 -> 33; placed +0.24 both; priced -0.13 C, +0.05 B), and stays under the v49 rerun by 3 stretch crossings on C and 1 on B, losing c-c2-s0010 on core C: the estimate on hard2 is still 13.8 items off on the mean (the stretch and edge streams carry 0.8-0.9 of the container's volume, not 0.54), so "over the estimate" is still under the real threshold there. Not adopted.

Verdict: no form of the estimate earns its place on the bench, because in the shipped form the low phase already overrides it where it matters; prior 42 and the `max` form are both crossing-neutral on the four suites against the v49 rerun, within the noise on placed, and attributed nothing by the deterministic pairs. `count_mode_items_per_container=42` is the one change the official samples support (threshold 22 on B and C, the samples' own count) and costs nothing measurable (core C 47 -> 47, core B 48 -> 48, det core 48 -> 48); it acts only on a board outside the low phase at the 21st item of a one-ULD stream, where v49 runs the resort without the count pass. Recommendation: ship `count_mode_items_per_container=42` on B and C as a correction, not as a measured gain; keep `count_mode_stream_volume` off (field kept for a lever gated on the estimate, e.g. a cover margin, which would need it); keep `count_cover_margin` off. Residual risk of 42: a platform stream of exactly 41 a container on two or three containers (threshold 42 / 62 read as 43 / 64) runs the count pass one or two items past the threshold outside the low phase: covers of priority on the boards where it finds no other pose (det core: the two-container scenes moved by -6 .. +2 items, no crossing lost).

**The gate alone** (`gate0-*`: soft as structure from the first soft box, `soft_structure_floor_share=0`, the 0.7 m cap kept, no selector; against the v49 form, and against the concurrent v49 rerun `estbase-*` where the runners were loaded; `gate0det-hard2` the deterministic hard2 C pair against `bandntdet-hard2`):

| suite | crossings (v49 / concurrent) | placed | soft covered | settle endings | priced |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core C | 48 -> 48 / 47 -> 48 | +0.52 / +0.79 | 45 -> 20 | 1 -> 1 | +0.35 / +0.31 |
| core B | 48 -> 48 / 48 -> 48 | +0.08 / -0.19 | | 1 -> 1 | +0.13 / +0.23 |
| hard2 C | 35/11 -> 33/9 ; 33/11 -> 33/9 | +0.3 / -0.3 | | 3 -> 4 | +0.24 / +0.16 |
| hard2 B (two runs) | 35/11 -> 35/12 and 35/12 ; 33/12 -> 35/12 twice | +1.3 / +2.2 and +1.6 / +3.5 | | 10 -> 5 | -0.02 / +0.14 and -0.00 / +0.16 |
| hard2 C deterministic | 36/11 -> 37/9 (s0010, s0014, s0021, s0036 in; s0004, s0007, s0040, s0059, s0076 out, all within 2 of their thresholds) | +0.40 / +0.22 | 59 -> 66 | 1 -> 3 | -0.04 |

The gate carries the floor's gain without the cap's cost: the sample suites keep every crossing with C +0.8 placed and the soft covers halved, hard2 B gains two sample crossings in both runs with placed +1.6 to +3.5 and the settle endings halved, hard2 C is flat (the deterministic pair nets -1 crossing on 96 scenes with +0.4 placed, the lost ones at the threshold's edge). Shipped as v50 on B and C with the count threshold's prior at 42.

**v50 and the official sample C scene.** The sample check of the gate-only form read C at 23.64 / 24 against v49's 26.44 / 25. Four repeats of task 002 alone per form: v49 (gate 0.35, prior 41) 25 / 25 / 25 / 25; the gate alone 25 / 25 / 24 / 24; the gate with the prior 42 25 / 25 / 24 / 24; v49 with the prior 42 25 / 25 / 24 / 25. So the flip is the gate form's timing sensitivity on that scene (a budget-cut decision going either way), not the prior; both outcomes cross the scene's threshold of 22. The prior 42 is not kept (no bench crossing moves with it, and the one scene it can change is this one).

**Standing soft cargo on the floor** (`stand-*`: `soft_standing_floor`, `max_freestanding_ratio_soft=3.0`, the own-kind selector's least-footprint pick, on top of the gate-only form; against `gate0-*`). The official simulator stands every sample soft class on its smallest face alone and in a row under the shake proxy (shift under 1.3 cm; `scratchpad` probe), and the ladder had never offered those poses for soft (its floor rungs add the standing orientations for hard cargo only, and the free-standing rule refuses height over base above 2.0, where the standing soft classes are 2.2-2.8).

| suite | crossings | placed | soft covered | shake topples | priced |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core C | 48 -> 48 | +0.12 | 20 -> 27 | 12 -> 21 | -0.04 |
| core B | 48 -> 47 (b-c2p-s0002 47 -> 40 against 42) | -0.40 | | 13 -> 24 | -0.04 |
| hard2 C | sample 33 -> 36, stretch 9 -> 11 | +0.19 / 0.00 | 80 -> 52 | 82 -> 56 | -0.22 |
| hard2 B | sample 35 -> 36, stretch 12 -> 11 | -0.54 / -1.44 (against the replicate -0.83 / -2.75) | | 113 -> 97 | -0.06 / -0.08 |

Not adopted: the sample suites are flat with a crossing lost on B and the shake topples doubled (the standing boxes that stood alone in the probe fall on the real boards' uneven soft neighbours), hard2 B is flat to negative; the one signal is hard2 C's +5 in a single loaded run, which a deterministic pair could attribute if the line is reopened (the overrides are 0.15 a scene, so most of the effect is the generators' new standing poses taken by the rungs themselves).

## **The platform refit: stability counts the episodes, and the crossing channel is exhausted**

A deep-dive over every build with a platform result and bench records (v18 to v49; `reports/wedge/platform-refit.md`, the full tables). The one finding that reorders the levers: every platform stability score from v1 to v49 is 4.175 x k for a whole number k (29 of 31 builds within 0.2; v24 +0.51 and v49 -0.84 the exceptions), and k steps by exactly one at every documented single-crossing event (v19, v25, v34, v41, v44 lost one; v31 gained three; v43 gained one). k is the platform's count of episodes over the threshold: 15 at v18, 18 from v31, 19 from v43 on, and the placed fraction (0.6475) sits at or above the sample suites' (0.633), which no mixture with hard2-like streams (0.50) can reach. So the platform's streams are sample-like, every one of its 19 episodes has counted since v43 (N = 19 is the simplest reading; N <= 23 at most), and a crossing lever can only lose (-2.34 a crossing). v49's +11 / +9 hard2 crossings bought no platform crossing; v44's best hard shares came with a lost one.

The continuous components, refit per counted episode (v31 on): fill tracks the bench fill at 0.6; cog is k times a per-episode value that moves 1.4 times the bench's cog_c; stability tracks k only (not topples, not cog); placement has been flat at 49.45 since v45 whatever the bench's credited priority share did (the count pass's covers), and moves only with k and Task A; soft follows H1 in structure but at less than half the bench's share rate, and v49's +4.2 is explained not by the suite deltas (17 times under) but by the structural price with k fixed: about one more credited soft box per B/C episode (100/15 x 2/3 = 4.4 soft points). Prices in total points (v31 on): a platform crossing 2.34; +1 bench fill point 0.17; -0.01 CoM ratio on all three tasks 0.30 (the old 1.7 came from v19's step, which was a lost crossing); one uncovered soft box in one platform episode 0.05; a priority box 0.15 (not realised by any B/C lever since v31); topples no reliable price. Headroom in total points: cog 7.7 (but the bench's cog moved 1.9 points in 18 builds), soft 7.6 (the one component that responded at the structural rate), placement 7.2 (frozen), fill about 17 by geometry.

Predicted v50 (`gate0-*` against `bandnt-*`, A unchanged): about +0.2 (fill +0.06, cog +0.29, stability +0.28 if v49's -0.84 was the B topples the band added, placement +0.22, soft +0.21), range -0.1 to +0.7 on the continuous terms, -2.3 per platform crossing lost.

## **The hard2 misses sorted into seven causes: volume, strips, door-side sealing, the soft carpet**

A deep-dive over the recorded hard2 runs (v49 and v50, C and B, the B replicate and the deterministic pair; `reports/wedge/hard2-miss-taxonomy.md` with the scripts and the per-episode rows under `reports/wedge/hard2-miss-taxonomy/`). Every miss board was rebuilt from its settled poses and sorted by a fixed rule order into one cause.

| cause (v50) | C episodes (items short) | B episodes (items short) | within 3 C / B |
|---|---|---|---|
| A volume out of reach (fill at the threshold > 0.506, the highest need any crossing ever met) | 13 (583) | 12 (536) | 0 / 0 |
| H no level patch left: floor in strips, tops closed | 14 (60) | 7 (27) | 6 / 4 |
| G a level patch is left but sealed from the door | 9 (79) | 14 (137) | 2 / 4 |
| D soft carpet (soft footprints >= 40 % of the floor; small-first / soft-first orders) | 10 (149) | 8 (125) | 3 / 0 |
| B ended by the simulator (settle / transport) | 3 (29) | 4 (130) | 0 / 0 |
| C a container left under-used | 2 (38) | 0 | 0 / 0 |
| E/F a reachable patch left untaken | 2 (26) | 3 (34) | 1 / 0 |

What this settles:
- **A quarter of the misses are volume misses and carry 55-60 % of the missing items**: all 8 fill-only and 5 of the 8 edge scenes need more fill than any crossing ever reached (p90 of the final fill is 0.46). No placement change recovers them. The hard2 edge and fill-only strata are not a target.
- **The geometry misses all end the same way**: fill 0.35 with 0.65 of the volume free, 30-36 % of the floor free but in strips 0.19-0.35 m wide, usable tops 0.00-0.05 of the floor, and the volume still needed only 0.07 of the container. The declining C call on an H board takes 0.6 s: there is nothing left to search. **One more item needs a different board, not a better last call**, and the near misses (margin -1 to -3) are closed boards with two exceptions out of twenty.
- **The course does not separate the causes** (Phenomenon 14 again): the largest one-step usable-surface drop is 0.15-0.19 of the floor some 11-20 placements before the end in misses and crossings alike, and the rescue share is 0.13-0.29 everywhere. Time pressure sits only in the soft carpets (6 over-budget steps an episode) and the untaken-patch cases; the strip boards have 0.3.
- **The soft-early orders are the costly ones**: small-first crosses 4 of 10 and soft-first 7 of 12 in reach on C, against soft-last 10 of 11 and big-first 5 of 6; above need 0.44 they cross 0 of 6. The soft-edge rung makes the largest usable-surface drop in 6 of 10 C carpets and 8 of 8 B carpets. The gate (v50) trims the carpets from 16 to 14 misses but the carpet survives it.
- **What the pool buys B**: +5 crossings on the same seeds (sample +2, stretch +3). On 7 of the 14 C strip boards one of the next ten stream items has a reachable spot. B's own misses are mostly sealing (G, 6 of 8 B-only): at its final decline B usually still holds level patches it cannot reach from the door (26 of 48 B crossings end that way too), so door-side sealing is B's late normal, not a miss signature. On C a sealed spot is specific to misses (18 of 49 misses against 6 of 40 crossings).
- **Clock noise**: 5 of the 15 v50 C sample misses occur only under the clock (3 settle endings after fallback plays, s0036, s0042) and cross in the deterministic run; one goes the other way. b-hard2-s0037 settles at step 9 in all three B records (a terrace-extension pose kept after a 5 cm, 4 degree wobble in the shadow check); c-hard2-s0091 ends on transport at 92 of 296 in every record.
- **A tool defect**: `scratchpad/surface_traj.py` (the Phenomenon 14 trajectory) marks every box hard and reads containers 1 and 2 without the container offset, so its multi-container ratios are container 0's. The taxonomy's trajectory corrects both.

Read with the platform refit: the platform's streams are sample-like and every platform episode already counts, so none of these causes is a platform crossing to gain. The taxonomy matters for what it rules out (the last-call search, the stretch and edge strata, the course signals) and for the one structural reading that carries over to sample-like streams: soft cargo placed early on the floor closes the board (D), which is the same cargo the refit prices as uncovered soft boxes at height.

## **Task B's time budget: the seconds go to stages that find nothing, and 6.5 s would cut episodes on the platform**

A deep-dive over the recorded B runs with local stage timers, counterfactual replays of the near-miss declines, and one CI measurement of `policy_budget_seconds=6.5` (`reports/wedge/task-b-time-budget.md`; records `reports/bench/bt65-core-b`, `bt65-hard2-b`; scripts under `reports/wedge/task-b-time-budget/`).

- **Where the time goes.** Calls are cheap until about 20 items are on the board; from 40 items the core-b median call is 4.4 s because the low phase is reached and most placements come from the rescues. Rescue placements (14 % of core-b's placements) start only after the budget is spent: their calls take 3.8 s or more, median 4.92 s. Inside a call that ends in a rescue or a decline, the ladder (about 2 s) and the stack option (about 1.2 s) spend 3.2 s and find nothing; the resort gets about 0.5 s plus 1 s of shadow checks.
- **The declines are not time-bound.** No near-miss decline ends at a rescue deadline. Replaying the six near misses at the declining call with 4.5 / 6.5 / 8.0 / 30 s: four are dead ends (no legal grid pose for any pool item, nothing to check), two place only at 30 s (s0007 by the physics resort after 10 checks at 14.2 s, s0040 by the count pass at 14.5 s) and neither at 6.5 or 8.0 s, because a larger budget goes to the ladder and the stack option while the resort's window moves later without getting longer.
- **6.5 s measured** (`bt65-*` against `gate0-*`): core-b crossings 48 -> 48, placed 39.56 -> 39.21 (-0.35), priced -0.04; hard2-b flat within the two baselines' own spread (sample 35 -> 36, stretch 12 -> 11), settle endings 4-5 -> 7, topples 113 -> 125. Policy time max 7.0 -> 9.2 s on core-b, 10.0 s on hard2-b. Under the platform's 1.5x machine factor 32 of 48 core-b and 52 of 96 hard2-b episodes would have a placing call time out (48 -> 41 crossings on each); 1.2x still cuts 4 and 15. **Keep 4.5 s.** The old v18 reading (+0.15 items at 8 s) predates the rescue stages. 8.0 s was not dispatched.
- **Bench fidelity caveat.** The B (and C) bench arms lack the shipped `stack_deadline=True` (in the submission's OVERRIDES since v44). Every bench call over 10 s is a stack-option call on b-hard2-s0070 / s0091 without that deadline, and 110-113 of the 118-130 hard2-b placements without a shadow check come from it. The bench's policy-time readings on B overstate the shipped form's; the arm files should carry the setting before the next time reading.
- Unmeasured ideas that are not budget changes: hand the low phase's ladder and stack-option time to the rescue stages (targets the two time-bound near misses, which needed about 14 s, so the gain is small); treat declining calls as free to overrun (a timed-out call fails like a decline; only placing calls carry the time risk).

## **v50 on the platform: 56.04, soft by its upper case, and two refit claims corrected**

v50 scored 56.04 (v49 55.62, +0.41; `reports/submission/official-results.md`): soft 46.5 -> 50.1, placement 49.45 -> 50.4, cog 64.25 -> 64.82, fill 39.65 -> 38.62, stability 78.48 -> 78.19, placed fraction 0.6475 -> 0.6446, policy time 6.75 s. The refit had predicted about +0.2 in a range of -0.1 to +0.7 with soft as the swing; the total landed in the range and soft moved by the refit's upper case (+3.6, about 0.8 of a credited soft box per B and C episode at k = 19), which confirms the structural rate as the way to price soft levers: judge them by uncovered soft boxes added per episode, and expect the platform to pay more than the bench's 48-episode suites show (the bench read half a box on sample C and a loss on B).

What the composition corrects in the refit:
- **Fill and soft trade against each other.** Fill fell 1.03 with the fraction flat, so the columns credit soft boxes where hard volume stood: the soft term (+0.51 weighted) outpays the fill term (-0.30). A soft lever's price must carry the fill it displaces; the bench's fill (+0.10 on C) did not show it.
- **Placement is not frozen.** It moved +0.95 with no Task A change and no crossing, so the placement term has a B/C component (the priority cargo's placement under the soft columns). The refit's "placement moves only with A or a crossing" is withdrawn.
- **Stability's continuous part can go either way.** 78.19 is 19 x 4.175 - 1.14; the gate did not remove v49's -0.84 (the refit had priced +0.28 for that) and added 0.3 more. The lattice (k = 19 of 19) holds; the crossing count is unchanged.

v50 is the shipped form. The next soft lever is priced at the structural rate net of the fill it displaces, and the sample C soft covers (20 left of the original 45) are the first count to move.

## **Bridges: the two-point-support pose exists, is dead in the ladder, and the rescue cannot afford it**

The question was whether a box can rest on two columns of one height and span the dip between them (`reports/wedge/bridge-spots.md`, scripts and outputs under `reports/wedge/bridge-spots/`). It can, by the packer's own rules: the `plateau-merge` rung spans two hard tops grouped within 6 cm and the stability criterion is the centre of mass inside the convex hull of the contacts, with no contact-share rule. In practice the rung places 1 of 1851 items on core C, 2 of 1900 on core B and 25 / 31 of about 4500 on the hard2 suites, because its anchor generator offers one centre per pair of tops (the gap's middle, the shared interval's middle) with no slide: over six core C scenes 1191 anchors gave 7 legal candidates (805 overlap a neighbour, 185 settle outside the container, 153 hit a shelf) and all 7 were vetoed later.

The spots are real. A scan of every declining board in the v50 records (two-point support within 1 cm of one level, the centre straddled by 4 cm or more on both axes, headroom, the door sweep clear) finds a reachable spot that the full-support level-patch check misses on 7 of 48 core C declines, 10 of 47 core B, 16 of 89 hard2 C (8 of 49 misses) and 21 of 91 hard2 B (14 of 43 misses); the agent's own validator accepts at least one of those poses on 5 / 1 / 9 / 13 of them and the grid rescue's 0.5 contact filter passes 4 / 1 / 7 / 10. The shape is not the shallow "n / n-15 / n" dip (once in 275 boards) but a box bridging the slot between two columns 0.5-1.3 m tall (median dip 0.94 m) at a contact share of 0.2-0.6.

The rescue reaches them only with time C does not have. Replaying c-c1-s0003 (margin 0) and re-asking the declining call: 4.5 s declines (grid rescue 1 try), 6.5 s declines (5 tries), 8.0 s places by the grid rescue at 7.8 s exactly the scan's bridge (bottom 0.86 m, 0.27 x 0.75 x 0.56 standing, contact 0.48, dip 0.82 m, among 10 grid poses) and the shadow world accepts it; 7.8 s is over Task C's platform limit. The other core C re-asks found nothing under 12 s (count pass at 12.8 and 17.8 s).

Price and verdict: one item on about a tenth of the sample-like declining boards (fill, soft where the item is soft; about +0.02 to +0.05 total), no platform crossing (k is 19 of 19), one or two hard2 near misses. Cheap to build (slide the bridge anchors along the gap at 2 cm; or give the grid rescue its window before the ladder's 2 s and the stack option's 1.2 s in a declining call; or drop the rescue's 0.5 contact filter to the count path's 0.25), and worth doing only as an addition to a soft-surface lever. The miss taxonomy's level-patch check undercounts these boards (its "no level patch" includes them); the correction does not change its ranking.

## **The bridge rescue: the low phase's rescue reserve is a Task C time lever, costs B a crossing, and the contact filter is neutral**

Two flags on the declining call's rescue path (`reports/wedge/bridge-rescue.md`; records `reports/bench/bresc-*`; scripts, arms and replays under `reports/wedge/bridge-rescue/`), measured with `stack_deadline=true` added to every arm and to the concurrent baselines. `grid_rescue_min_support` lowers the grid rescue's 0.5 contact filter (the old support-ratio rule; the validator's convex hull decides stability) and changes nothing measurable: with the phase switch on, every low-phase call already takes the count path's 0.25. `low_phase_rescue_seconds` was built as "the ladder's and the stack option's time to the rescue" and redesigned after the stage timers. On Task C the low phase's ladder (one item, 0.2-0.5 s) and stack option (0.1-0.4 s) are cheap; the grid rescue was starved by the count pass's failed shadow checks (about 0.45 s each, nine in c-c1-s0003's decline). So the reserve also goes to the grid rescue out of the resort's window.

The replays place c-c2p-s0001's declining call inside the budget (grid rescue, 4.8 s at 1.5 s of reserve, 2.5 s at 2.0), where the base declines even at 8 s. c-c1-s0003's bridge stays out of reach: the grid tries its ten covers in contact-share order and the bridge (0.36) is the 7th.

| arm (vs concurrent base) | core C crossings | core C placed | C placing calls > 5.3 s | C 1.5x cut episodes | core B crossings | core B placed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| base (v50 + stack deadline) | 48 | 37.98 | 13 | 11 (45 crossings left) | 48 | 39.35 |
| filter 0.25 | 48 | +0.33 | 7 | 4 | 48 | +0.02 |
| reserve 1.5 s | 48 | -0.08 | 1 | 1 (48) | **47** | +0.15 |
| both | 48 | +0.38 | 0 | 0 (48) | **46** | +0.44 |

- On C the reserve is a time lever, not an items lever. The slowest call goes 6.30 -> 5.26 s and the grid rescue's placements 5 -> 58-107 a suite, with no crossing lost. hard2 C (both flags): sample 33 -> 34, stretch 9 -> 9, placing calls over 5.3 s 95 -> 8, against a record without the stack deadline.
- On B the reserve loses b-c2-s0002 (50 -> 41, the same in both arms). With the filter, b-c2p-s0002 (45 -> 40) goes too. The low phase covers most of a B episode, so a ladder cut from 2.7 s to 1.8 s changes the course. B's grid rescue already had its 1.5 s (`grid_rescue_pool_seconds`). hard2-b is flat within its two baselines.
- To ship on C only, a gate was added after the runs: `low_phase_rescue_single=True` acts with a pool of one and reproduces the base on B. Proposed B/C overrides: `grid_rescue_min_support=0.25, low_phase_rescue_seconds=1.5, low_phase_rescue_single=True`. The platform refit prices no crossing to gain; what C gains is the tail of placing calls near the 8 s limit.
- Next cheap step toward the bridges: order the grid rescue's poses by the centre of mass's margin inside the contact hull instead of the contact share.

## **Sliding bridges measured: the generator works, the spots are mostly overhangs, and no form pays**

The bridge generator the bridge deep-dive asked for was built and measured (`reports/wedge/bridge-generator.md`; scripts, arms and analytic outputs under `reports/wedge/bridge-generator/`; records `reports/bench/bslide-*`, `bslate-*`, `bsresc-*`, `bsresc0-*`). Flags, all off by default: `bridge_slide` (anchor step along the slot between any two structure tops at one level, hard tops and shelf tops, flush positions and the crossing range's ends included, the validator's overlap rule vectorised over all anchors, the floor and plateau vetoes asked before the family quota), `bridge_slide_max`, `bridge_slide_orientations`, `bridge_plateau_exempt` (a bridge on two separated supports is exempt from `no-plateau-to-build-on`), `bridge_slide_late` (the sliding bridges as the ladder's last rung, yielding to the next container), `bridge_slide_rescue` (as the call's last stage). The old rung's 1191 anchors gave 7 legal candidates on six core C scenes; the slide offers about 220 raw and 17 clear anchors a call at unchanged call time.

What the generator's own reading corrects in the bridge deep-dive: asking the stability model what each validator-accepted scan pose rests on, every accepted pose on core C, core B and 6 of 9 hard2 C boards rests on one top, an overhang with the centre of mass inside that one patch; the scan's 1 cm "tight" cells had counted lower neighbours. Two-contact accepted poses exist on 5 boards, 4 of them hard on soft or priority cargo (the cover rule forbids them) and one over 0.08-0.12 m^2 of bare floor (the floor veto). The lever is an overhang generator.

| form | core C crossings | C placed vs recorded / vs concurrent base | C bridges (episodes) | C soft covered | core B crossings | B placed | B bridges | B soft covered |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| v50 recorded / concurrent base | 48 / 48 | - / -0.42 | 1 / 1 | 20 / 22 | 48 | - | 2 | 6 |
| slide on the plateau-merge rung + exempt | 48 | -0.06 / +0.35 | 22 (18) | 22 | **47** (b-c2p-s0002 -9) | -0.79 | 59 (28) | 14 |
| slide as the last rung | 48 | +0.04 / +0.46 | 9 (8) | 28 | 48 | +0.08 | 4 (4) | 3 |
| slide as the call's last stage, with / without the exemption | 48 / 48 | -0.25 / -0.23 (recorded) | 6 / 3 | 28 / 26 | 48 / 48 | -0.94 / +0.19 | 18 / 2 | 5 / 7 |

hard2 (head-rung form): C sample crossings 33 -> 35, stretch 9 -> 7, bridges 195 in 53 episodes, soft covered 80 -> 48; B crossings flat, placed -0.5 / -2.0. Analytic (ladder-only, 48 core C): head rung 1405 -> 1390 placed (a surviving bridge outranks every floor rung and lids slots early), last rung 1405 -> 1409 with no crossing changed.

Not adopted. The head rung loses a core B crossing; the last rung keeps every crossing and reads inside the noise on items while covering 8 more soft boxes on core C (the bridges lid the soft tops the refit prices); the last-stage forms are flat to negative. Nothing ships; the flags stay in the code for the record. Together with the rescue measurement (the low phase's reserve is a C time lever that costs B a crossing; the contact filter is neutral), the bridge line is closed: the ragged-floor spots are real but worth one item on a handful of sample-like boards at a soft-cover cost.

## **The stability lattice re-read: the constant is too tight for N = 19, and four or five platform episodes may not count**

Over all 33 scored builds, stability / round(stability / 4.175) is 4.1775 with sd 0.0051 (0.12 %) once v24 and the two builds with known topple residuals (v49, v50) are left out, from v1 (placed fraction 0.50, k = 11) to v46 (0.64, k = 19); it does not move with the placed fraction or the official samples' placed counts, and it moves with the bench's topples per episode (-0.165 per topple an episode). A shake-test quantity cannot be the same to 0.1 % across builds whose topple rates and placed counts vary by 30 %, so for a counted episode the stability score is a fixed credit, 4.1775 = (100 / N) x s. N = 19 needs s = 0.794 for every episode of every build, which no physics produces; N = 23 needs s = 0.961 (one unstable box in 25, or the failing last action in the denominator); N = 24 needs full credit plus a 0.1-0.3 excess. Under the surviving readings **k = 19 of 23 or 24, so four or five platform episodes are not counted** (`reports/wedge/stability-lattice.md`). The refit's objection from the placed fraction (the counted episodes would have to average 0.675) assumes the fraction is a plain mean over all N and that an uncounted episode placed half or fewer; the README says neither (the count rule is "a certain number", the half is our fit; the run's status string names `is_included`, `is_placed_safe`, `is_valid`). One diagnostic submission that surrenders every Task B item at step 0 reads N_B, whether every B episode was counted, the aggregation rule of the fraction and the fill, and the per-episode cog / placement / soft of B, all from one platform result; a second one on C completes the picture. Until then the count lever's platform value is unknown, not zero, and the quality terms' headroom is smaller than the N = 19 reading made it (per-episode cog / placement / soft about 78 / 61 / 61 at N = 23).

The bench's v50 arms differed from the shipped `submission/agent.py` config in `stack_deadline` (off on the bench, on in the ship), `rescue_order_gate` (`low` on the bench, so Task B ran the deep-first / sealing pass that the shipped `low+single` keeps to C) and `soft_headroom_volume_share` (0.75 against 0.9); the arms that match the shipped form field for field are `reports/bench/arms/arm_ship_c.txt` / `arm_ship_b.txt`, and their records are `reports/bench/ship-core`, `ship-core-b`, `ship-hard2`, `ship-hard2-b` (physics, budget 4.5, same seeds as `gate0-*`).

| suite | gate0 arm -> shipped form | placed | policy max |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| core C | 48 -> 48 | +0.00 | 6.12 -> 6.11 s |
| core B | 48 -> 47 (b-c2-s0002 50 -> 41, threshold 42) | -0.58 | 7.03 -> 7.41 s |
| hard2 C | sample 33 -> 34, stretch 9 -> 12 (s0021 settle -> +5, s0059, s0065, s0069 in; s0030, s0036 out) | +0.67 / +1.28 | 14.87 -> 6.16 s |

C is unaffected except that the stack deadline removes the bench's 10 s calls (the shipped form never had them). On B the shipped form is below the bench's reading: one sample crossing and four hard2 crossings, with the hard2-b replicate's own spread at two swaps. The suspects are the sealing pass that `low+single` turns off on B and the larger soft headroom; two ablations of the shipped B arm (`rescue_order_gate=low`, `soft_headroom_volume_share=0.75`) on core-b and hard2-b are dispatched (`shipseal-*`, `shiphead-*`). Every B verdict since v44 was read on the bench arm, so the B side of v48 (the gate kept off B as "mixed") and of v50 is to be re-read against these records.

## **The Task B diagnostic: 11 B episodes counted, the means are plain, N is about 23, and the uncounted episodes are in A or C**

The build surrendering every Task B item at the first call scored 26.31 against v50's 56.04 (`reports/submission/official-results.md`; `reports/wedge/stability-lattice.md`, section 6). The stability drop is 10.98 lattice steps, so 11 Task B episodes were counted in v50 and 8 A or C episodes remain counted, with v50's -1.2 residual staying (the topples are in A or C). The placed fraction fell to 0.347, which no mean over counted episodes can give, so the fraction and the fill are plain means over every episode. With 11 B episodes placing a sample-like 0.60-0.65, the fraction's drop puts N at 22-24 (N = 19 would need B at 0.51 and A/C at 0.82 with a fill of 48): about 23 episodes, 12 of them A or C, 8 of those counted. **Three to five platform episodes in A or C are not counted; B is fully counted or at most one short.** Per counted B episode (N = 23) cog 77, placement 48, soft 51. The platform's policy time is a plain mean (6.75 -> 5.07 s with B's 0.7 ms calls), not the slowest call. The Task C diagnostic splits A from C.

## **The shipped B form's two ablations: the sealing pass restores the bench's crossings, the headroom share does not**

Against the shipped-form B baselines (`ship-core-b`, `ship-hard2-b`), the shipped B arm with the deep-first / sealing pass back on B (`rescue_order_gate=low`, records `shipseal-*`) and with the old soft headroom share (`soft_headroom_volume_share=0.75`, `shiphead-*`):

| arm | core B crossings | core B placed | core B policy max | hard2 B sample / stretch | hard2 B placed (sample / stretch) | priced (continuous) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---:|
| shipped B form | 47 | 38.98 | 7.41 s | 34 / 9 | 43.58 / 47.97 | - |
| + sealing pass on B | 48 (b-c2-s0002 41 -> 50) | +0.50 | 7.08 s | 35 / 11 (s0014, s0040, s0053, s0076 in; s0048 out) | +0.62 / +1.78 | -0.17 (cog) / -0.08 |
| + headroom share 0.75 | 47 | -0.58 | 7.33 s | 36 / 9 (s0007, s0014, s0040 in; s0021 out) | +0.27 / +0.59 | -0.04 / +0.01 |

The sealing pass is what the bench's B arm had and the shipped form lacks: it accounts for the lost sample crossing and three of the four hard2 crossings, with more items and a lower slowest call. It does not pay on the continuous reading (cog -0.17 on core B), and the Task B diagnostic says every platform B episode is already counted, so a B crossing buys nothing there; v48's decision to keep it off B stands on the platform's terms, and the bench's B readings since v44 were taken on the better-crossing form. The headroom share moves nothing that survives the hard2 replicate's spread.

## **The Task C diagnostic: 3 C episodes counted, 5 A episodes counted, and the N question is a Task A question**

The build surrendering every Task C item scored 47.33 against v50's 56.04: the stability drop is 2.78 steps with the topple residual moving -1.19 -> -0.27, so 3 Task C episodes were counted in v50 (and carried the residual), which with B's 11 leaves 5 counted Task A episodes (`reports/wedge/stability-lattice.md`, section 7). The per-task shares of v50 are A fill 14.7 / cog 18.9 / placement 17.1 / soft 17.4 / fraction 0.255, B 18.4 / 36.6 / 23.2 / 24.4 / 0.298, C 5.5 / 9.3 / 10.2 / 8.4 / 0.092. Every N from 19 to 24 is arithmetically open; B is fully counted in all of them and C has at most one uncounted episode. What separates the readings is Task A: no uncounted episode (N = 19) needs the five A episodes at 97 % of their manifests and a fill of 56 (the bench's A suite: 0.635 and 37.5, best scene 0.78 / 47.8; the official sample A 25 of 41 at 32.1), while N = 22-24 has A with 8-10 episodes of which 5 count at sample-like fractions (0.61-0.73, fill 35-42), the other 3-5 zeroed on the four components although they place far more than half, so by a validity condition rather than the count. Per counted episode A has the highest cog (72-90), placement (65-82) and soft (66-84) of the three tasks. The platform's A manifests are, by the optimize time, single containers of 80-110 items, a regime the bench has never run; whether the A pipeline can place 97 % at a fill of 56 there decides N, and that is being measured on the bench (`stability-lattice/afill.py`).

## **Why Task A episodes fall under the count: the soft cargo is planned last and left out, and the dense manifests cannot be crossed by volume**

The Task A pipeline (the v50 arm of `adrop-core-a`) on dense single-container manifests in physics (`reports/wedge/stability-lattice/afill.py`, `afill.json`): 60 items place 28 / 28 / 30 at a fill of 40.7 / 44.6 / 44.4 against a threshold of 31; 100 items place 25 / 25 at 36-40 against 51. The load tops out at a fill of 40-45 with the tops at 1.2-1.5 m of a 1.57 m ceiling. So N = 19's requirement for the platform's A (97 % of the manifest at a fill of 56) is out of reach of this pipeline, and the reading is N = 22-24 with 3-5 Task A episodes under the count on manifests like these (the v40 note put the platform's A manifests at single containers of 80-110 items).

Where the count goes: on the 60-item seed 2 manifest the planner places the hard cargo smallest first (volume ranks 33-44, then 18-27), the soft cargo last, and 5 of the 21 soft items get in; 16 soft boxes of 45-73 L, the smallest items of the manifest, stay out while 3 items were missing for the count. The hard-A suite's 21 misses (`adrop-hard-a`) show the same shape: in 14 of 21 the unplaced soft cargo alone outnumbers the margin, and in 14 the smallest missing items would need at most 48 fill points in total (the pipeline's maximum); the soft placed share on the misses is 0-30 % (s0019 5 of 69 soft, margin -2; s0047 18 of 59, -4; s0032 2 of 27, -9; s0027 5 of 39, -10). The rest (s0020 need 0.70, s0030 0.56, s0037 big items only) are volume-bound.

The platform's per-episode A quality is the best of the three tasks (cog 72-90, placement 65-82, soft 66-84 across the readings), so the A lever is the count alone: a count-secured plan for manifests the plan does not hold (the plan's items under the threshold plus `plan_value_margin`): the smallest items of every class first, the soft cargo in its own block (columns to 0.7 m, or a gallery along one wall with the hard rows elsewhere), hard rows after the count is secured, the plan judged first by its count. Measure on hard-a (27 of 48), a dense-A suite of single containers with 50-80 items, and core-a (48 of 48 to hold). Each platform A episode recovered is about 2.3 points.

## **Count-secured planning for Task A: the soft block loses to standing rows, and the re-plan under the threshold doubles the dense crossings with core-a held exactly**

`plan_count_secure` (`rule_alpha/count_secure.py`, `reports/wedge/a-count-secure.md`; all flags off by default) re-plans a Task A manifest whose planner plan holds fewer than the count threshold plus `plan_count_secure_margin`, and ranks the plans first by the count gate. Two re-plans were built. The count frame the diagnosis proposed (the threshold plus two smallest items, the priority cargo always in it, its soft cargo as a block on a floor strip, its hard cargo in rows beside it, the rest after) holds fewer items than the ordinary plan or as many on ten dense manifests (left strip 266, back 251, right 229, no block 196, against 265): soft carries soft only to the 0.7 m cap and over a 35 % floor share, and a top in the mid-height transport band is not carried in, so a soft block is two layers, about 11 items a square metre, where a column of the smallest hard boxes holds 18-27. The soft boxes are the smallest items by volume, not by floor. What holds the count is the row planner with `plan_standing_rows` and `plan_soft_own_rows` (308, 5 of 10 over the threshold against 1), the lever v19 measured on the sample suite and dropped for its soft and priority cost; gated to plans under the count it costs nothing that counts.

| arm (physics, CI) | dense-a crossings | hard-a crossings | core-a crossings | core-a soft / priority placed | core-a CoM | topples dense / hard / core |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|---|
| shipped A (`ship-*-a`) | 10 | 26 | 48 | 381 / 255 | 0.326 | 8 / 19 / 4 |
| re-plan under thr + 2 (`acount-*`) | 21 | 31 | **47** | 369 / 246 | 0.341 | 3 / 20 / 8 |
| re-plan under thr (`acount0-*`) | **20** | **29** | **48** | 381 / 255 | 0.328 | 5 / 17 / 4 |

The re-plan under the threshold itself (`arm_acount0_a.txt`: `plan_count_secure=true, plan_count_secure_choice=true, plan_count_secure_race_replanned=true, plan_count_secure_margin=0, plan_count_secure_blocks=plain;top`) changes one core-a episode (a-c1-s0012, plan 19 under the threshold, 21 -> 26 placed) and loses no crossing anywhere: dense-a +2.5 items, fill +3.4, soft covered 37 -> 10, priority placed +26; hard-a in s0021, s0038, s0047. Under the threshold plus 2 the re-plan reaches core-a's crossing plans (9 of 48 sit in that band), raises their loads (CoM +0.015, priced -0.84 on the continuous reading) and, through the race's count rule, hands a-c1s-s0006 to a dry-run plan that replays to 19 of the 21 needed. By count, dense-a crosses at 50 items 8 -> 10, 60 2 -> 8, 70 0 -> 2, 80 0 -> 0: the best plan reaches 32-36 items in the c1 container against 41. So the platform value turns on what its uncounted A episodes are: about half cross if they are 50-70-item manifests, none if they are the 80-110-item single containers the v40 note read from the optimize time (about +2.3 points an episode crossed). Proposed for Task A: the five `acount0` fields; `submission/agent.py` is unchanged.

## **v51: Task A's count-secured re-plan shipped**

The count-secured plan's threshold-only form (`reports/wedge/a-count-secure.md`: dense-a crossings 10 -> 20 of 48, hard-a 26 -> 29, core-a 48 of 48 with its soft and priority counts unchanged, no topple added) ships as v51 in the `task_a` overrides alone; Tasks B and C are v50's. The official sample is unchanged (A 32.11 / 25: its plan is over the threshold, so nothing re-plans; B 29.59 / 26, C 26.44 / 25). The platform reads whether its 3-5 uncounted Task A episodes are of the 50-70-item kind the re-plan crosses or the 80-110-item kind nothing here crosses; the stability lattice (4.1775 a counted episode) will say how many came over.
## **The typed-standing plan: the count frame's soft and priority cargo standing against the back wall crosses five dense-A and four hard-A manifests more**

`plan_typed_standing` (`reports/wedge/a-standing.md`): when the ordinary plan holds fewer than threshold + `plan_value_margin` items, the planner also plans the count frame (every priority item, then the smallest items up to the gate) with its soft and priority cargo first, on the floor, on the smallest face in rows against the back wall (braced by the wall and the first hard row in front), and the hard rows in the rest of the floor; the plan that holds the gate, then scores higher, plays. The official-simulator probe (`scripts/standing_rows_probe.py`): every soft class and the standing priority A/B boxes stand on their smallest face with a settle shift under 0.7 cm and no topple under the shake in any arrangement, braced or open; a standing box cannot be placed behind a hard row already standing (refused on the way in), so the gallery is planned first. On paper the gallery trades hard columns for soft boxes about one for one (one item per 0.09 m^2 of floor against the columns' 0.075; the c1 container's plan holds 25-31 items whatever the manifest), and forced on every manifest it costs dense-a 2.4 items a scene.

| arm (against the shipped A arm, `ship-*-a`) | dense-a crossings | hard-a crossings | core-a | topples dense / hard / core | soft placed dense / hard |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gated, `back-stand-2;back-stand-1;front-stand-2` (`astand-*`) | 10 -> 15 | 26 -> 31 | 48, placed +0.15 | 8 -> 2 / 19 -> 20 / 4 -> 10 | 6.2 -> 7.7 / 8.1 -> 11.0 |
| gated, braced only (`astand2-*`) | 10 -> 15 | 26 -> 32 | 48, placed +0.15 | 8 -> 1 / 19 -> 20 / 4 -> 10 | 6.2 -> 7.7 / 8.1 -> 10.7 |
| braced only, no priority-container layouts (`astand3-*`) | 10 -> 15 | 26 -> 30 | 48, placed +0.29 (6 up, 0 down) | 8 -> 1 / 19 -> 17 / 4 -> 6 | 6.2 -> 7.7 / 8.1 -> 9.7 |
| braced gallery forced everywhere (`astandfb-*`) | 10 -> 8 | 26 -> 30 | | 8 -> 1 / 19 -> 10 / | |
| gallery at the opening forced (`astandff-dense-a`) | 10 -> 13 | | | 8 -> 27 (priority covered 23 -> 86) | |

No standing gallery box toppled in any episode. The braced gallery lowers the load (CoM -0.011 to -0.014 forced) and the topples; the unbraced one triples the topples and covers the priority cargo. The added core-a topples are hard boxes high on stacks (c1s-s0003 +2 typed; the c2p scenes +4, where the flag also cost 3 items each, hence `plan_typed_standing_no_priority_container`). Against the A ship rule the `astand3` form holds core-a's 48 and its placement and soft shares within two priority boxes (0.781 -> 0.771) but adds one topple episode (4 -> 6), so it is not adopted as the rule is written; the gain (+5 dense, +4 hard crossings at about 2.3 platform points each) is a ship candidate for the coordinator's call. Dense-a's c1x70/c1x80 manifests stay out of reach of any plan of this pipeline (best 33 placed against 36/41).

## **v51 on the platform: 53.50, one counted episode lost, and why the re-plan cannot be judged on the bench's budget**

v51 (v50 plus Task A's count-secured re-plan under the count threshold) scored 53.50 against v50's 56.04: stability 73.99 = 18 x 4.1775 - 1.2 against 19 steps, so one counted episode was lost and none gained; cog -3.61, placement -1.95, soft -3.50, fill -0.30, fraction 0.6446 -> 0.6391; the optimize time rose 118.9 -> 138.8 s against the 140 s budget, the policy time unchanged. The four gated components' drops price one lost A episode (about 2.7 points at A's per-episode values); the extra -0.5 of soft and the fraction's -0.0055 are the other re-planned A episodes placing less soft. On the bench's core-a the re-plan fired once in 48 episodes; on the platform's dense A manifests it fired on most (the 20 s of optimize time), and v50's base planning already takes about 119 s there, so the re-plan variants were cut by the deadline or never started. The code then still marks the episode re-planned, and the count race (`plan_count_secure_choice` with `plan_count_secure_race_replanned`) decides the planner-vs-dry-run race by the count gate: a dry-run plan holding the gate beats the planner's plan, which is the failure mode the threshold + 2 trigger showed on core-a (a-c1s-s0006, the dry-run plan placing no soft or priority box). That is the reading of the lost episode; the bench never showed it because its optimize budget is never short. Not kept: the shipped form stays v50's. Next: the race switch only when a re-plan variant actually completed and won, the re-plan run before the remaining base variants (or a reserved share of the budget), and the bench emulating the platform's budget with `offline_budget_seconds=40-60` on dense-a and core-a before any second try.

| suite | shipped | count-secured (v51) | typed standing | both |

| hard-a | 26 / 46.8 / 389 / 19 | 29 / 48.0 / 500 / 17 | 30 / 47.2 / 466 / 17 | **31** / 48.4 / 521 / 18 |
| core-a | 48 / 37.5 / 381 / 4 | 48 / 37.7 / 381 / 4 | 48 / 37.8 / 403 / 6 | 48 / 37.9 / 404 / 6 (priority 255 -> 253) |

The combination is the bench's best A form (dense-a 23 of 48, the soft count up everywhere, topples down on dense-a), with the typed gallery's two core-a topples and two priority boxes. It inherits v51's platform flaw (the count race under a cut re-plan on a slow machine; the bench's optimize mean is 46-86 s where the platform's base planning takes about 119 s of 140), so it is a v52 candidate only after the re-plan's race and budget fix is measured under a shortened offline budget.
## **Soft cargo as a floor under hard cargo on Task A: the physics holds, the planner gets two crossings in 144 episodes**

The official simulator first (`scripts/soft_floor_probe.py`, 148 cases with a hard-for-soft control; `reports/wedge/a-soft-floor.md`, `a-soft-floor/probe-table.md`): hard boxes on soft boxes survive placement, settle and the shake when the soft cargo is a level block of whole rows: a soft floor two to four rows deep under three to five hard layers (tops 0.94-1.45 m) settled within 3.4 degrees and toppled nothing, like its hard control. What does not stand: two soft storeys under hard cargo (sinks 4-9 cm, 3 topples in 12), a single soft box under a column of two or more hard boxes (E, G: 2-4 topples or a settle failure), a single soft row under three or four layers of B (6-8 topples), the 0.45 x 0.30 E box under C or H (22 degrees), soft heights more than 3 cm apart under one hard box (5-24 degrees). The soft boxes sink 1.2 cm into the floor plane alone and 2-7 cm under hard layers, unevenly by 1-3 cm; a soft box on the floor is outside the evaluator's fill inclusion even alone, as on every C record.

The plan (`plan_soft_floor_under_count`, off by default; `rule_alpha/planner.py` `_soft_floor_*`): under the count gate the manifest is planned again with one storey of soft boxes of one height band from the back wall and the hard rows on it (the cover rule waived for the floor alone, the headroom reserve off), every hard pose over the floor legal both on the floor sunk 3 cm and unsunk; the replay reads the floor's uneven tops within 3 cm, the online board reads the floor as hard cargo once the plan plays, and the plan plays only when it holds the threshold plus the margin itself. Against the shipped records (`ship-*`), third CI round (`asoftfloor3-*`) and a flag-off control on core-a (`asoftfloor-off-core-a`): core-a 48 -> 48 (against the control: placed +0.15, soft share 0.454 -> 0.462, one priority box less, 2 soft boxes covered, CoM +0.003); hard-a 26 -> 29, of which the flag's own +2 / -1 (s0047 41 -> 58 over 49 with 21 soft boxes covered, s0029 9 -> 16 over 16; s0048 22 -> 20 under 21) and the rest run-to-run variation; dense-a 10 -> 11 (c1x50-s0002 25 -> 28). The plans that used the planned sink to fit a third layer under the transport band at the shelf level replayed to half their items (34 planned, 15 placed); planned robustly, the soft floor reaches the count on a handful of manifests, never on the 70-80-item dense ones, where the smallest items up to the threshold need a fill of 0.38-0.45 and the pipeline plateaus at 28-29 items.

Not shipped: the gain is soft-heavy hard-suite manifests, and the platform's uncounted A episodes are, by every reading so far, sample-like 80-110-item single containers the variant does not reach. The flag and the probe stay; the next forms to try are a soft storey whose height keeps the hard layering clear of the band, and soft boxes in the strip the hard rows leave rather than under them.
## **After v51 (53.50, one counted A episode lost): the count race kept to chosen re-plans, the `top` plan dropped, and a short-budget bench that does not reproduce the loss**

v51 shipped the threshold-only re-plan and lost a counted Task A episode (stability 19 -> 18 steps; cog -3.61, placement -1.95, soft -3.50; optimize 118.9 -> 138.8 s of 140). The fix (`reports/wedge/a-count-secure.md`, section 6; `arm_acountfix_a.txt`) has three parts. `plan_count_secure_race_chosen` lets the count rule decide the planner / dry-run race only after a re-plan completed and was chosen, and hands an episode to the dry-run's plan only when the planner's plan is under the threshold itself; v51's rule acted wherever the base plan was under the trigger. `plan_count_secure_early` runs the re-plan right after the first plan. The `top` plan is dropped: it doubled the re-plan's time, and on 2 of 48 dense episodes it was picked holding the count with no priority box. Every episode record now carries `metrics.count_secure` (the re-plan's variants and pick, the race's decision against the score's pick).

The bench's mean optimize time is 2.5-3 times shorter than the platform's, so 40 and 60 s budgets stand in for its 140 s. At both, the shipped form, v51 and the fixed form read: dense-a 10 / 20 / 20 crossings (placed 28.10 / 30.65 / 30.69); core-a 48 / 48 / 48 with soft 392 / 392 / 392 and priority 262 / 262 / 262 at 40 s. The re-plan was never skipped or cut (plain 5.7-6.2 s, at most 10.1 s). The race's count rule never overrode the score's pick (dense-a 7 decisions, all for the planner, which the score also picked; core-a none). So the bench does not reproduce v51's loss. What it does show is the shipped race's own variance between arms that decide the same: a-c2-s0011's dry-run plan (58 items, no soft, no priority) beats the planner's 47 in some runs and not others (51 placed with 0 / 0 against 50 with 11 / 7), and a-c2p-s0008 at 140 s goes from soft 11 to 3. That variance accounts for the fixed form's core-a soft 370 against 381 at the full budget. The candidates for the platform's loss are the `top` pick's dropped priority cargo (removed), the re-plan's time taken from the dry-run on the slower machine (halved), and that race variance (unguarded; `plan_prefer_planner` is the existing guard).
